# Deferred backlog — unified tracking

The single index of deferred / not-yet-fixed work. It consolidates items that
were previously scattered across `docs/changelog.md` ("Review findings recorded
but NOT fixed"), the `post-retrofit-tasks` memory, the milestone memory, and
per-session checkpoints. **This document is canonical going forward** — add new
deferrals here; the source lists remain as historical record.

**Snapshot:** `main @ 3c0a2de`, 2026-07-21 (after the 2.4.0 fold + closeout:
PR #8/#9/#10 merged, PR #4 closed). None of the items below block 2.4.0.

> **FILE-WIDE RETRACTION, 2026-08-13 (X-51).** Rows throughout this file describe
> the 60 s `PreToolUse` budget as a **"fail-closed ceiling"** and read a crossing
> as an over-denial — *"a refusal of a command v2.7.2 permitted"*. **That
> characterisation is retracted everywhere it appears here.** A hook cancelled at
> its declared `timeout` exits 124/137/143; only exit 2 blocks; the call
> **proceeds**. Crossing the ceiling **skips the gate**, so every such crossing is
> a **bypass**, not a refusal. The *measurements* in those rows stand and are not
> re-stated — only their direction was wrong. Retracted once here rather than
> edited into ~9 historical rows, so the record of what was measured when stays
> intact. See `docs/agentic-harness-security-kb.md` §4.11.

Status legend: `open` (actionable work) · `decision` (needs an owner call before
work) · `no-action` (reviewed, judged fine as-is; listed so it isn't re-derived)
· `done` (resolved; kept for provenance).

## A. Owner decisions (no code until decided)

| ID | Item | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| A-1 | Emitted-gate fail-open posture under a total parser outage | **DECIDED 2026-07-28 → fail-closed** (v2.6.0, upstream P0-3b). Blocking gates exit 2 with a reason when neither `jq` nor `python3` exists; advisory hooks declare `FAIL_CLOSED=0` and degrade to a logged no-op. `tests/test_retrofit.py` T2.FS7b re-pointed to the new posture. `done` |
| A-2 | Accept-or-schedule this deferred set | Formally treat as deferred-not-forgotten, or queue clusters. `decision` |
| A-3 | Shell-parity judgment call (Milestone B) | SDK gates stricter than the 2.0.0 shell gates on edge cases; parity is a follow-up PR if wanted. `decision` |
| A-4 | Trajectory pruning | `purge_old_state_after_days` has no consumer; auto-deletion unimplemented (new destructive behavior). Partially addressed at v2.6.0 — `.decision-pending-*` is swept on a 7-day window and `hooks.log` rotates at 1 MiB (upstream P3) — but trajectory files under `.claude/logs/` remain unpruned. `decision` |
| A-5 | **Should a `retrofit` install fail closed during its warn-only weeks?** (lens B finding 5) | **REOPENS the retrofit half of A-1.** A-1 was closed for greenfield at v2.6.0 and that half stands: the report demonstrated an executed fail-open in a security gate, and leaving it open pending a decision means continuing to ship it. What was *not* in scope was deciding it for **retrofit**. On `mode: retrofit` with `ROLLOUT_WEEK: 1`, a parser outage now blocks `spec-gate-commit`, `test-gate` and `tdd-gate` — in a week `RETROFIT.md:1250-1255` says blocks **"Nothing (warn-only mode)"**, for the reason the same document gives at `:1246`: day-one blocking *"produces immediate friction that drives bypass behavior"*. A brownfield operator whose week-1 commits are refused with `BLOCKED (fail-closed): unexpected hook error` is precisely the trainer R8.A.6 exists to prevent. **Mechanically available either way:** the ordering is the cause — the retrofit preamble's first act is `CMD="$(jget …)"`, so `hook_fail` fires before `retrofit_should_block` is consulted, but `ROLLOUT_WEEK` is read with `grep -oE` from `rollout-schedule.md` and needs no JSON parser, so it could be resolved first and `FAIL_CLOSED=0` set for a hook the schedule says is warn-only. `secrets-gate` stays fail-closed unconditionally either way (`RETROFIT.md:1134`). **Practical severity is bounded and should be said plainly:** `python3` is a hard prerequisite of the installer, so any machine that ran `bootstrap-install` has a parser; the realistic trigger is a stripped hook environment. **NOT IMPLEMENTED — current behavior (fail-closed in all weeks) is left in place pending the owner's call**, because silently deciding an open owner decision is the exact criticism lens B makes of the previous round. Related disclosure, currently recorded nowhere in the emitted tree: under a *total* parser outage every `PreToolUse` gate exits 2, so every `Bash`/`Read`/`Write`/`Edit`/`Grep`/`Glob` call is refused and the harness is inoperable with no bypass path. That is the posture the upstream report asked for, but an operator should be told it exists | `decision` — **owner** |
| A-6 | **What should `spec-gate-commit`'s predicate actually be?** (lens B finding 6, and the upstream report's own escalation) | P1-2 had **two** structural failures. Failure 1 (the bootstrap commit is impossible — the gate blocked its own `INDEX.md`) is fixed by `ENFORCED_PREFIXES`, now on both substrates. Failure 2 is **not fixed and is not fixable by scoping**: *"the first **code** commit is impossible — `spec-decompose` deliberately produces tasks and behaviors, not filenames, so no source path is ever in the corpus."* Scoping to `src/` does not soften that; it *targets exactly* the files a behavior-oriented task corpus will never name. Executed against a realistic post-`/spec-decompose` corpus, a staged `src/auth.gleam` is still refused. The upstream report escalated this as *"a design question for the maintainer, not just a patch"* and it still is. The shape of the question: should the gate match on **paths** at all, or on something a behavior corpus does contain (spec slug ↔ directory convention, a task-id trailer in the commit message, an explicit `covers:` line in `tasks/*.md`)? Each is a protocol-level convention change, not a hook edit | `decision` — **owner** **[Layer 2026-10-05, WP3 (branch `feat/wp3-spec-workflow`, not yet merged): decided by D2.]** The owner's D2 (2026-10-04) keeps paths and makes the corpus name them: `/spec-decompose` now writes task files whose `Files:` block lists every file the task changes, and the check (`.claude/githooks/spec-check.sh`, run by installer-written git hooks for agent commits) covers a path only through such a list or a directory entry above it. Failure 2 above is gone on that branch: a commit of a listed file passes. The check ships in warn mode (operator ruling 2026-10-05), so no commit is refused by default; the switch to block is Z-28. **[Layer 2026-10-13, the WP3 closeout.]** The previous layer's "not yet merged" was true on its date: WP3 merged as `042d88a` (PR #122, 2026-10-13T12:44:10Z), so D2's check is on `main`, still in warn mode (Z-28). |

## J. v2.6.0 upstream-fix residue (2026-07-28)

Sources: `docs/bootstrap-protocol-upstream-bugs-2026-07-28.md` and the two
adversarial lenses run against the fixes themselves —
`docs/lens-a-execution-findings-2026-07-28.md` (F1–F10, execution) and
`docs/lens-b-execution-findings-2026-07-28.md` (findings 1–15, spec conformance
and regression).

**Correction, 2026-07-28.** This section used to open with *"Every P0/P1/P2/P3
finding in that report was fixed at v2.6.0."* That was false when written:
P1-2's second structural failure, P2-4's Under half, and P2-5's
reason-distinguishing half were not fixed, and cluster J recorded none of them.
The two-lens batch fixed most of the residue and the rows below are what it
deliberately left. The rule the false sentence violated is worth stating: a
cluster whose purpose is *"what those fixes deliberately left"* must not
contain the word "every" about work it did not measure.

| ID | Item | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| J-1 | **Quoted-argument verbs no longer match.** `sh -c "git commit"` and friends were caught by the old substring matching; anchoring to command position (P1-4) drops them. Accepted deliberately — the alternative is every false positive the report documented | **SURFACED 2026-07-29 (round-4) — this row now describes NEITHER substrate and retiring it is an OWNER call, not an implementer one.** Round 3 gave the shell's `cmd_has_verb` invoker re-segmentation and did not give it to the SDK's `_git_verb`, so `sh -c 'git commit -m x'` was shell=deny / SDK=allow — a divergence in the direction `sdk_gates_template`'s own binding rule forbids. Round 4 made both substrates DENY it (`_git_verb` now calls `_expand_invoker_args`), because leaving them disagreeing was not an option and allow was the fail-open side. **That is a substrate-parity fix, not a decision to retire J-1**: the recorded trade-off said an invoker's quoted argument should stay unmatched to avoid false positives, and the round-4 model says an invoker's argument is a command line. Both cannot hold. The corpus pins the rows `OPEN` rather than `want=deny` for exactly this reason | **DECIDED 2026-07-30 (v2.6.0 release review): yes, `sh -c "git commit"` IS a commit.** The round-4 model wins and the report's accepted trade-off is retired. Rationale: both substrates already deny it, the alternative direction is the fail-open side, and reverting would re-split the substrates that round 4 deliberately reconciled. Executed at review time — `sh -c`, `bash -c`, `ssh host "…"` and depth-3 nesting all deny on test-gate and ci-mirror; `sh -c "git status"` and `echo "git commit"` still allow, so the false-positive property anchoring bought is intact. **The v2.6.0 PRD §6.D item stated the retired trade-off as current fact and is corrected** — the checklist now requires the invoker rule rather than recording its absence. Residue for a future batch: the emitted hooks' shared header comment ("KNOWN AND ACCEPTED LIMITATION") and the corpus rows still carry the old model; neither is operator-facing, both should be re-pointed at this decision | `done` |
| J-2 | `ENFORCED_PREFIXES` (spec-gate-commit, P1-2) is an editable constant baked into the emitted hook, not a `bootstrap.config.yaml` field. The obvious mitigation — skip segments with an unbalanced quote — fixes it in the **fail-open** direction and was declined. *(Note the deliberate asymmetry with `secrets-gate`, which after lens B finding 4 does the opposite and treats a quoted run as one token. Both premises assumed a ONE-half rule. | J-16 | **`test-gate` lost run-once-per-source-change idempotency, and a heredoc body still triggers it.** Removing the forgeable `.last-test-pass` marker (lens A F4) was right — whatever the gate can compute, an agent holding Write can compute and write — but nothing replaced the *idempotency* the marker also provided, so the suite now runs on every commit attempt and every retry, between `git add` and `git commit`. A test command that rewrites a staged file therefore makes the commit record stale content. Separately, `cmd_segments` treats a heredoc body as ordinary command text, so writing a CONTRIBUTING.md or release script that merely *contains* a `git commit` line runs the whole suite and blocks the write if it is red. Residue: `.last-test-pass` is still emitted into `.claude/.gitignore` for a file nothing creates. A pattern of `touch /tmp/x` then runs on every hook invocation. With a non-executable pattern the ERR trap converts it into a permanent `rc=2` on every payload — a total DoS no test catches. Present at **every commit in this chain**, so not introduced by any review round, but unrecorded until now and in the same emit path as the original P0-1 RCE. `lib/installer.py` validates `never_read_paths` nowhere. The config is operator-authored, so this is privilege-equivalent rather than a privilege escalation — but the installer should still refuse a pattern containing the heredoc sentinel, a newline, or a leading `-` | **FIXED 2026-07-29 (round-4 D12), and the "privilege-equivalent rather than a privilege escalation" framing is what made `open` look defensible for three rounds.** The framing is also wrong about the blast radius: the config is operator-authored, but the emitted hook runs on EVERY tool call, so a poisoned entry is a persistent execution primitive, and the pattern array is silently TRUNCATED so the protections the operator asked for stop existing with rc=0 and no warning. All four validate at `resolve_config` now | `done` |
| J-18 | **`eval-gate`'s two substrates diff different ranges.** The shell diffs `HEAD~1`; the SDK diffs `@{u}..HEAD`. With two unpushed commits and the prompt file in the OLDER one, shell allows and SDK denies — a binding-rule violation, present at every commit in this chain and in both directions depending on history shape. The differential's eval-gate section has no `deny` rows at all, so it can see neither | **FIXED 2026-07-29 (round-4 D4).** Each substrate had a range the other lacked, which is why reading this as "the shell is the complete one" would have left half of it open: the shell had the root-commit `git ls-tree` branch and no upstream range; the SDK had `@{u}..HEAD` and no root-commit branch, so on a shallow clone (`actions/checkout` defaults to `--depth 1`) it saw an empty diff and ALLOWED. Both now carry all three ranges in the same order, and the SDK's `prompt|\.md$` predicate is narrowed to the shell's `*[Pp]rompt*|prompts/*|*/prompts/*` so a docs-only push stops being denied on one substrate only | `done` **Corrected 2026-10-04 (WP2, via X-36z); stays `done`.** The 2026-07-29 "FIXED" was true of the SDK only. The shell's `@{u}..HEAD` branch was emitted with doubled braces and could not run, so the shell-allows/SDK-denies divergence this row records stayed live until WP2. Now fixed on both substrates, with differential deny rows. |
| J-19 | **The empty-payload refusal overrides a hook's declared posture.** The `hook_fail` on empty stdin sits in the shared header ABOVE each hook's `FAIL_CLOSED=0` line, so `cost-log` (Stop), `task-done-alarm` (SubagentStop), `decision-required-alarm` (Notification) and `drift-detector` (PostToolUse) all exit 2 on an empty payload despite declaring themselves advisory. `exit 2` does not mean "fail closed" on a `Stop` event — it means "do not stop" — so one exit code was applied across five event semantics. Consequence is reasoned, not executed: no live Claude Code runtime was available to any round-3 lens | **FIXED 2026-07-29 (round-4 D17), and the count was wrong: it is SIX, not four.** `format-lint-gate` (PostToolUse) and `spec-gate-entry` (**UserPromptSubmit**, where `exit 2` blocks the USER'S OWN PROMPT) were missing from the list. The posture is now baked into the shared header at emission time, DERIVED from the body's own `FAIL_CLOSED=0` declaration, so there is no second list of advisory hooks to drift and a new advisory hook gets the right header automatically. Asserted directionally in `tests/test_hook_behavior.py`: an advisory hook must never return 2 on an unusable payload, a blocking gate must. The *consequence* half (what each event semantics does with each exit code) remains reasoned rather than executed — it needs a live Claude Code runtime, which no round has had | `done` |
| J-20 | **No test compares the tokenizers against a real shell.** Every one of the five review rounds has shipped a `cmd_segments` / `_sg_scan` defect, and every round's clean tokenizer result came from a hand-built corpus written by the same person who wrote the code — round 3's lens A swept 2202 generated commands and found nothing, then found two fail-opens by hand, because the generated corpus contained no multi-line quoted runs. A generative differential against `bash` word-splitting over a few hundred thousand random commands is the only mechanism any lens could name that reaches the class | **SUPERSEDED 2026-07-29 (round-4), and its PRESCRIPTION was wrong.** A differential against `bash` word-splitting would NOT have caught D1 or D2: both tokenizers agree with bash about word-splitting and disagree about command POSITION, which is downstream of it. What reaches the class is a COMPOSITION sweep — `wrapper x wrapper-flag x operand x invoker x quoting x separator x verb`, at gate level, on both substrates — now committed as `tests/composition_sweep.py` with a bounded sample running every commit in `tests/test_composition.py`. The diagnosis in this row was right (five rounds, five hand-built corpora, five missed defects); only the remedy was wrong | `done` |
| J-15 | **A nested quote inside a shell invoker's argument keeps its quote character on the word.** The round-2 quoted-run rule re-tokenizes an invoker's argument (`sh -c`, `bash -c`, `ssh`, …) so `sh -c 'cat secrets/prod.yaml'` blocks again, but the re-tokenization is a whitespace split, not a second parse — so `ssh h "git commit -m '.env'"` yields `'.env'` and over-matches. Over-match on a command that runs a shell *remotely* is the cheap direction, and the frequent case (ordinary prose in a `git commit -m`) is protected by the invoker test, which `git` fails. Closing it means recursing the tokenizer into the run, which re-opens the question of how deep to follow nesting | **REWRITTEN 2026-07-29 (round-4 D8) — as written this row was INVERTED, and it told every future reader the residual failed safe.** It claimed `ssh h "git commit -m '.env'"` OVER-matches and accepted that as the cheap direction. Executed at `b1782ec`, its own example **ALLOWS** — there was no over-match. The real behaviour was the mirror image and it was a FAIL-OPEN in the gate this repo repeatedly calls the one with no override path: `bash -c "cat secrets/prod.yaml"` denied while `bash -c "cat 'secrets/prod.yaml'"` **allowed and read the file**. One extra pair of quotes was the whole bypass, because the re-tokenization was a whitespace split and left `'secrets/prod.yaml'` glued to its quotes. Fixed by re-PARSING the invoker's argument through the scanner (depth 3, invoker test re-applied at each level) on both substrates. **The over-match this row originally described now genuinely exists**: `ssh h "git commit -m '.env'"` denies. That is accepted for the reason the row gave — the alternative is a fail-open on remote installs — and it is recorded in `docs/round-4-intended-relaxations.md` R-4b with the corpus row (QOT-34) re-pointed in the same commit | `no-action` — the trade-off is real NOW; it was not when this row was written |
| J-13 | **`npx`/`uvx`/`dlx`/`exec` blocking is new false-positive surface.** The two-lens batch newly blocks the run-without-installing channels (lens A F3 residue), which means `npx tsc` on a local devDependency now needs an entry in `deps.md`. That is the intended posture — the gate exists for "unapproved software arrives" and not installing first is not a mitigation — but it is the row most likely to generate an operator complaint, so it is recorded rather than discovered | `no-action` — revisit if it proves noisy in practice |

## K. Round-4 remediation residue (2026-07-29)

Scope of that round was **greenfield only** (owner decision, 2026-07-29).
Behaviour changes are enumerated up front in
`docs/round-4-intended-relaxations.md`; anything not on that list and not below
was a fail-open close.

| ID | Item | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| K-1 | **`mode: retrofit` was OUT OF SCOPE for round 4, and D14 is still open.** The retrofit legacy allowlist is inert in production: the preamble calls `retrofit_is_allowlisted "$TARGET"` on the raw `jget` value, while the F-1393 absolute-path normalization sits in the greenfield body *below* it. On a real retrofit install with `legacy_allowlist: ["src/**"]` and `ROLLOUT_WEEK: 4`, `src/legacy_thing.py` is exempt (rc=0) but `/abs/proj/src/legacy_thing.py` is refused (rc=2) — and absolute is the shape the harness actually sends. **This has recurred across three review rounds** for a structural reason: `mode: retrofit` has NO golden fixture and ZERO rows in the substrate differential, so it is untested by construction. Round 4 treated `tests/test_retrofit.py` (263 checks) as a TRIPWIRE only, and it stayed green | `open` — fix needs a retrofit golden fixture first, or it will recur a fourth time |
| K-2 | **`ci-mirror` has no SDK twin, and "substrate parity" is scoped to 7 of 11 gates.** `_GATE_FACTORIES` carries 7 gates against the shell's 11. Shell-only: `ci-mirror`, `drift-detector`, `cost-log`, `task-done-alarm`, `decision-required-alarm`, `spec-gate-entry`. `ci-mirror` is the one that matters — it blocks `git push` on CI failure and has **zero rows in the substrate differential**, because there is nothing to compare it against. Under `gate_substrate: sdk-callable` dispatch, `git push` is UNGATED. The emitted module's own comment says so; what was missing is that every "the substrates agree" claim in this repo silently means "the seven that both have" | `open` — either port `ci-mirror` or state the 7-of-11 scope everywhere parity is claimed |
| K-3 | **`secrets-gate` is still LINEAR in payload size, and its timeout fails CLOSED.** Round 4 made it ~2.8x cheaper on the exposed shape (per-pattern derivation hoisted out of the candidate loop; candidates de-duplicated), measured on one machine at 15.15 -> 5.43 ms/line with a 202-pattern config, which moves the 60 s `PreToolUse` crossover from ~4,000 lines to ~11,000. The MECHANISM is unchanged: a large enough Bash payload is still a hard block on benign input, in the gate with no override path. The quote-heavy single-huge-command shape the gate's own comment warns about was measured this round for the first time and is ~3x CHEAPER per unit than many-short-lines, so the linear many-lines shape is the real exposure. Closing it properly means an O(1) rejection pre-filter over the whole pattern set, which needs either `extglob` or a glob->ERE translation layer — both have their own bug surface | `open` — bounded, not closed; re-measure on the target machine |
| K-4 | **The known-defect ledger in `test_substrate_differential.py` is now EMPTY, deliberately.** Its one armed row (A10, unbalanced quote vs. the dotenv exemption) was fixed this round and retired, and the count pin moved 1 -> 0. This is the state the ledger's own comment warns about — round 3 shipped it inert, every row fixed, `ledger()` at zero call sites, and described it in the commit message as a standing guard. Recorded so the next reader knows 0 is a measurement, not a default. **Known weakness, unchanged:** deleting a row AND its count pin together is silent; the mutation tests cover either changing, not both | `no-action` — accurate as of 2026-07-29 |
| K-5 | **`WebFetch{url}` is inspected by no gate, and that is now an explicit decision rather than an omission.** Round 4 enumerated every parameter of every gated tool (D19) and added `Grep{glob}`, which was returning matching file CONTENTS through a sibling parameter of two that had already been fixed. `url` was decided the other way: it names a remote resource, not a local path, so `secrets-gate` has nothing to match it against. If a URL-shaped exfiltration channel is in scope, that is a new gate, not a new candidate | `decision` — is outbound URL an exfiltration surface this suite gates? |

## L. Round-6 / round-7 `settings.json` merge residue (2026-07-29)

Scope of those rounds was the **co-owned `settings.json` merge**, its uninstall
reversal, `verify_wiring`, the skip path and `main`. `build_plan` was untouched
throughout, so no emitted body moved.

| ID | Item | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| L-1 | **Should a symlink at a planned path BLOCK the install, and does the answer differ by file kind?** Round-6 F5 decided it for `settings.json` only: a symlinked settings.json is declined at rc=3 rather than replaced or written through, because a file shared between projects would gain `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/...` hook commands and every other project sharing it would then run scripts it does not have. Every OTHER planned path is still replaced. **The load-bearing half of that rationale does NOT transfer, which is why this was not closed by drive-by:** the generic write path is `os.replace`, so it swaps the LINK for a regular file and never writes *through* it — verified by execution; the link target keeps its bytes. The shared-file harm is therefore structurally absent there, and what remained was purely that it happened SILENTLY. **Round 7 fixed the silence only, deliberately leaving behaviour alone:** the per-file line names the link, an end-of-run block repeats it (a mid-transcript line is not a signal — a SKIP buried on stdout line 29 of 60 is exactly how the original unenforced-tree defect went unnoticed), `--dry-run` shows it in the future tense before anything is written, and the manifest records `replaced_symlink` so the displaced target is still findable once the transcript is gone. **The common case was already safe for an unrelated reason:** an *untracked* symlink is skipped because it reads as operator content, so the failure needs the narrow sequence tracked → symlinked afterwards → digest still matching → a config change that rewrites that file | `decision` — **owner, per-kind**. A symlinked hook script has a real argument for declining (a shared body would be one project's gate running everywhere); a symlinked steering doc mostly just blocks installs. A uniform rule papers over that difference, and `settings.json`'s rc=3 comes from `skipped_security`, which a doc would not trigger anyway |
| L-2 | **The legacy-migration sequence was reasoned through but never executed.** `_split_owned_hooks` accepts bare command strings from a pre-site manifest, and a legacy record is retired only when the emission no longer carries that command at all. The round-7 review traced the path — install at `844b8e0`, then run the newer installer over the same tree, with an operator foreign-site registration added in between, and again with a hook retired from the config in the same step — and concluded nothing of theirs should vanish, but did **not** run it. In a file where reading has repeatedly missed defects that execution caught, treat that as unexamined rather than clean | `open` — execute the three shapes; anyone already running this branch has such a manifest |

## M. v2.6.0 release-review residue (2026-07-30)

The review that cleared the v2.6.0 doc pair for tagging. Findings that landed in
the three documents are not listed here; these are the ones deliberately **not**
taken in that pass, because fixing them means changing the *emission* and a
documentation release should not carry an emission change.

| ID | Item | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| M-1 | **§6.D says `chmod 700` on the hooks directory; the installer emits `755`.** Executed: `.claude/hooks` is `755` and every `*.sh` is `755` on a fresh install, and no suite asserts the mode (`grep -rn '0o700\|chmod 700' tests/ lib/` is empty). Pre-existing — equally false at v2.5.0 — and the bullet was not edited at 2.6.0, so it was left in place rather than softened. Two ways to close it: tighten the emission to `700` (a real behavior change for any tooling that reads the directory as another user), or narrow the checklist bullet to what the installer actually guarantees. Prefer the former; the bullet is the correct posture | `open` |
| M-2 | **Emitted `drift-detector.sh` and `audio-alerts.config` cite `Bootstrap-Protocol-v2-5-0.md` §6.E.** Added by the v2.5.0 release review's honest-scope pass and not re-pointed at 2.6.0, so an operator reading their own emitted config is sent to the superseded document — and specifically to the one file whose bytes differ from its own tag. Cosmetic but self-defeating for a release that re-pointed every other self-reference. Part of the doc-reference-normalization pass the v2.4.0 history section already owes | `open` **done — WP4 (branch `feat/wp4-claude-md-steering-entry`, not yet merged), layer 2026-10-14.** The emitted `audio-alerts.config` now names the protocol as `templates.PROTOCOL_DOC` gives it, `Bootstrap-Protocol-v2-8-0.md` 6.E (g5-1a). `drift-detector.sh` already cited no `v2-5-0` file at `0845862`: its one protocol citation is `_HOOK_HEADER`'s `Bootstrap-Protocol-v2-0-0.md` 6.D line, which the 2026-09-27 pivot keeps byte-identical and the protocol-doc-snapshot-retire queue row records. **[Layer 2026-10-16, the WP4 closeout.]** WP4 merged as `6c771f2` (PR #125, 2026-10-15T17:31:43Z), so this fix is on `main`. |
| M-3 | **The invoker-rule residue from J-1's decision.** The shared hook header still reads "KNOWN AND ACCEPTED LIMITATION … `sh -c "git commit"` … no longer matches", and the differential corpus pins the invoker rows `OPEN` rather than `want=deny`. Both now contradict the decided model and the shipped behavior. Neither is operator-facing, which is why this is a residue row and not a defect | `open` |

## N. Coverage gaps surviving the 2.6.0 batch (2026-07-30)

Found while compiling `docs/agentic-harness-security-kb.md` by executing the
v2.5.0 and v2.6.0 emissions side by side. **None is a regression** — every row is
a scope limit the upstream batch did not reach, or a gap the batch created no
worse. They are filed because they were recorded nowhere: not in the upstream
report, not in clusters A–M, and until now only in the KB's prose. Every rc below
was executed on the v2.6.0 emission at tag `v2.6.0` (`f6bded0`).

| ID | Item | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| N-1 | **The `dependency-gate` refusal names a remediation that does nothing.** It prints "Approve in-session and update `.claude/steering/deps.md`", but the approved list is baked into the hook at emission time from `deps.approved` (`mapfile -t APPROVED <<'APPROVED_EOF'`). Editing `deps.md` changes no behaviour until the installer is re-run. **Compounding:** the installer `SKIP`s a locally-modified `deps.md`, so the operator who follows the instruction also stops receiving the new refusal-class documentation. This is the KB §4.5 class — an advertised remediation path that is inert — and is the most operator-hostile survivor of the batch: the operator does the thing they were told, observes no change, and concludes the gate is broken. Fix is a choice: read `deps.md` at runtime, or change the message to name `bootstrap.config.yaml` + re-install | `open` |
| N-2 | **`dependency-gate` still does not match five ecosystems.** Executed on v2.6.0: `mix deps.get` rc=0, `rebar3 get-deps` rc=0, `opam install x` rc=0, `nimble install x` rc=0, `apt-get install x` rc=0. (`gem install x` rc=2 — Ruby *is* covered.) The upstream report named `mix` and `rebar3` explicitly under P1-3 and the fix batch closed the others around them. Elixir/Erlang are the notable miss given the report came from a BEAM-adjacent project | `open` |
| N-3 | **`secrets-gate` never inspects file *content*.** Executed: a `Write` to `notes.md` whose body is `-----BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY-----…` returns rc=0. The gate is a path-policy engine only. Recorded by the upstream report under P2-4 and not addressed. Whether content scanning belongs in a `PreToolUse` hook at all is a design question — the honest interim move may be to say so in `secrets.md`, which currently implies broader protection than the path list gives | `decision` — owner |
| N-4 | **No project-boundary or traversal check.** Executed: `Read ../../etc/passwd` rc=0, `Read /etc/shadow` rc=0. Paths outside the project are evaluated against the configured patterns and nothing else. Named in the upstream report under P2-4, unaddressed | `open` |
| N-5 | **`WebFetch` is on no matcher.** The emitted matcher set is `Bash`, `Read\|Write\|Edit\|NotebookEdit\|Grep\|Glob`, `Write`, `Write\|Edit`. The upstream report named `WebFetch(file://)` as an unguarded read route under P0-2; the fix added `Bash` and the file tools and left `WebFetch`. Overlaps open decision **K-5** (is `WebFetch{url}` a gated exfil surface?) — K-5 asks the policy question, N-5 records that the read-side route is also currently open | `open` — pairs with K-5 |

## O. Autonomous-mode review residue (2026-07-30)

Found by the first round that ever **executed** the emitted wrappers
(`loop.sh`, `goal-loop.sh`, `auto.sh`) rather than reading them — the surface
three prior rounds covered only by byte assertion. The four defects that round
found are fixed and pinned by `tests/test_wrapper_behavior.py` (demonstrated to
fail, 11 checks, against the pre-fix templates); D-9 and I-8 are closed above.
These are what it recorded rather than fixed.

| id | item | status |
|----|------|--------|
| O-1 | **The completion and escalation sentinels have no reader.** Executed: every reference to `.loop-complete-<id>`, `.loop-halt-<id>`, `.evaluator-feedback-<id>` and `loop-final-<id>.md` in all three wrappers is inside a **comment**; zero executable statements. Consequences: Phase 9.6's "three independent signals" claim for terminal success is untestable against the artifact (none of the three is read); the urgent-escalation halt that Phase 9.7 calls **"not configurable"** routes through `.loop-halt-<id>`, which nothing reads. Not a regression — it is the skeleton's scope — but the docs describe these as active mechanisms, which is the KB §4.5 class. **The forward-looking half matters more:** Phase 9.6 specifies that terminal success *requires* the agent's own `.loop-complete-<id>` write and "is never inferred", so when the operator completes the loop per the wrapper comments, signal one of three is a file the agent writes and nothing re-verifies. Nothing in the emitted tree constrains that and no test asserts it | `open` — pairs with I-2 |
| O-2 | **The iteration-summary hook implements none of §6.C's contract beyond presence.** `Bootstrap-Protocol-v2-8-0.md:807` specifies presence **and format-validity**, a `summary_failure_count` counter, and three consecutive format failures counting as a `goal-condition-suspect`-class halt. Emitted: `[ -s ]` only — a one-byte file passes (executed). `grep -rn summary_failure_count lib/ bin/` returns nothing. The 2026-07-30 fix corrected the hook's exit code (1 → 2, so the demand actually blocks) and bounded it with `stop_hook_active`, but the counter is per-task persistent state the Stop payload cannot scope — it carries no task or iteration identity (see I-13) — so it belongs in the operator-completed wrapper beside `loop_in_flight`, not in the hook | `open` — pairs with I-13 |
| O-3 | **Should the `exit_reason` enum gain a skeleton value?** **DECIDED 2026-07-31: NO — and the question was the wrong shape.** The enum is queue-level contract surface answering *why a RUN ended*; "the dispatch loop was never written" answers *why no run started*, and a 14th value would have put an authoring state into a run-outcome vocabulary. The wrappers now set `REFUSAL` and log `REFUSED: <cause>`, claiming no enum value at all — so the enum stays at 13, the `tests/test_usage_limit_contract.py` pin is untouched, and no protocol doc changed. Blast radius was always one `hooks.log` line: executed, the skeleton writes **no** `queue_runs_history` entry and no run-summary. Cost was freeze-exception no. 30, `full_autonomous` only | `done` |
| O-4 | **An agent can lock the queue runner out by forging `.run-active`.** Executed: writing `pid=1 start=…` (init, always alive) to `.claude/queue/.run-active` makes every subsequent `auto.sh` refuse with "A queue run is already active (pid=1)". The stale-sentinel prompt never triggers because the pid *is* alive. The direction is correct — refuse rather than run concurrently — and the refusal names the file to remove, so this is availability, not integrity. Recorded because the file is agent-writable and gitignored, which is the KB §4.1 shape, and because a runner that cannot start on an unattended night looks identical to one that finished | `open` |
| O-5 | **Cross-mode claim has a ~10% mutual-refusal livelock.** The mutual-exclusion invariant **holds** — measured with a holding body, 30 concurrent loop+goal pairs on one task id: exactly-one 27, both-held **0**. The residual 3 are runs where *both* refused: each wrapper creates its own `O_CREAT\|O_EXCL` sentinel *before* taking the `flock`, so a tight interleaving lets each see the other's sentinel under the lock and abort. Availability, not safety, and self-clearing on retry. Fix if it ever matters: claim under the same lock that guards the cross-mode check | `open` |
| O-6 | **The KB's account of what the executing suite covers reads broader than it is.** `docs/agentic-harness-security-kb.md` §5 records `tests/test_hook_behavior.py` as the answer to "why detection failed" — payloads into the emitted **hooks**, asserting exit codes. True, but the wrappers are controls too (halt sentinels, eligibility refusal, the claim protocol) and nothing executed them until 2026-07-30, so a reader finishes §5 believing the executing-suite gap was closed when it was half-closed. §6's "Residual gaps — EXECUTED" does not name the wrapper gap either, and §7's first checklist item ("every blocking control has a must-deny and a must-allow payload, both asserted against the **deployed** artifact") is exactly what the wrappers failed. **CLOSED 2026-07-31.** `tests/test_wrapper_behavior.py` exists and §5 now cites it: the section records the wrapper gap, the four defects the first executing round found, and the follow-on lesson that the suite itself could report `0 passed, 0 failed` as `ok` on a flock-less host. §6 gained a post-tag block carrying P-1..P-4 and P-17, and the KB gained P0-3d (a fourth parsing fail-open) plus §4.6. **Self-reported by the round that added that suite** | `done` |

## P. Adversarial review of the cluster-O fix batch (2026-07-31)

The independent pass on the O batch that §5.1 of the KB says every fix batch in
this repo has needed. 8 attackers, per-finding refutation, completeness critic:
45 claims raised, **23 refuted**, 22 survived. Almost every refutation turned on
one axis — *the mechanism reproduces, but it is byte-identical at the parent, so
it is not a finding against this batch*. Worth reusing: "I reproduced it" is not
"this commit caused it", and a PRE/POST execution table is what separates them.

**Two blocking defects were found and FIXED, not filed:** the `stop_hook_active`
bound tested parser *presence* rather than *usability* (root cause was `jget` in
the shared header, which failed **every parsing gate open** on a broken-but-
present `jq` — see the changelog entry for 2026-07-31), and
`tests/test_wrapper_behavior.py` reported `0 passed, 0 failed` on a flock-less
host while `bin/run-tests` scored it `ok`. Rows below are the residue.

Ordered by operator impact. `pre-existing` marks a defect the O batch did not
introduce — recorded because it is live, not because it is a regression.

| id | item | status |
|----|------|--------|
| P-1 | **The wrapper fixes are silently withheld from exactly the installs that need them.** Executed: install at the parent, append one operator line to each wrapper (which their own stderr instructs — *"Implement the dispatch loop … before any unattended use"*), re-run the installer. Both **hook** fixes land unconditionally; all three **wrapper** fixes are skipped — `SKIP .claude/loop.sh (locally modified; use --force to overwrite)` — at `rc=0`, reported as a bare `skipped=3` counter in ~70 lines of `ok`, with nothing saying a safety change was withheld. So D-9/I-8's `done` markers are conditionally false for the only population where a runtime logging defect bites: operators actually running loops. The documented remedy, `--force`, overwrites their implementation wholesale; there is no merge path. Minimum: surface withheld files distinctly from ordinary skips, and qualify the D-9/I-8 rows | `open` |
| P-2 | **The demand releases without re-checking itself, so it is a one-shot advisory, not enforcement.** Executed, three consecutive Stops with the summary never written: rc=2, then rc=0 (`stop_hook_active`), then rc=2 on a fresh turn. The bound sits *above* the summary check, so turn 2 ends whether or not the agent complied. Combined with O-2 (no `summary_failure_count`), the gate costs one extra turn per iteration and enforces nothing across turns — while `goal-config.md` emits `summary_failure_halt_threshold: 3` promising a halt nothing reads. The exit-code fix made the gate *audible*, not *enforcing*; the docs should say which | `open` — pairs with O-2 |
| P-3 | **The durable record is inverted: the block logs nothing, the release logs.** Across the same three Stops, `hooks.log` contains exactly one line — `stop_hook_active; not re-blocking`. Every allow path logs; the exit-2 enforcement branch is the only silent outcome in the hook. A morning-after operator sees the gate declining to act, with no record that it ever acted. `pre-existing` in mechanism (the branch had no `log` at exit 1 either), but the O batch is what made that branch a *control action* rather than a no-op | `open` — `pre-existing` |
| P-4 | **Nothing in the emitted tree teaches the agent the summary contract.** Executed grep over a full install: `.iteration-summary-*` appears only in the hook itself, `.claude/.gitignore` and `settings.json` — not in `goal-config.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `goal-loop.sh`, or any skill or command. The path, name and required contents are stated exactly once, in the stderr of the block, which is delivered the turn before the bound stops enforcing it. An agent that has never been told the filename cannot satisfy the gate on the turn where satisfying it matters | `open` |
| P-5 | **A non-empty but unparseable payload still defeats the bound.** The `parser_ok` self-test proves a parser *works*; it does not prove *this payload parsed*. With truncated JSON, or valid JSON that is not an object, `jget` returns empty, `stop_hook_active` cannot read true, and the hook exits 2. Narrower than the broken-parser case now fixed (it needs a malformed payload from the harness, not a broken host) but it is the same shape and the same file. Fix: distinguish "parsed, key absent" from "did not parse" — the degrade branch already exists and is correct | `open` |
| P-6 | **The Stop gate is armed install-wide by a stale or forged marker, and nothing reaps them.** `SID` is computed and never read; the gate globs `.claude/sessions/.goal-active-*` with no session, task, pid-liveness or mtime scoping; the file is gitignored and agent-writable. After one hard-killed goal run, every ordinary interactive Stop in that project costs an extra turn. Bounded to one turn by the working bound, and the wrapper's refusal is self-announcing, so this is cost, not lockout — but the O batch is what turned it from `rc=1` into `rc=2`. Same shape as I-13 | `open` — pairs with I-13 |
| P-7 | **The revived tier-3 cooperation point over-fires on stale sentinels.** The glob split fixed the under-match; the globs stay project-scoped. One `.loop-active-*` left by a crashed run makes the branch fire in a session that is in no loop at all — pre-fix this needed two sentinels, post-fix one suffices, so the fix widened the stale-sentinel window it inherited. Advisory and `rc=0`, so the ceiling is noise; the hook is on `PostToolUse` with no matcher, so "noise" means every tool call while the sentinel exists | `open` |
| P-8 | **No wrapper has a reachable `exit 0` any more, so a zero-backoff retry supervisor now spins.** Executed: `until .claude/auto.sh; do :; done` terminated after one iteration at the parent and never terminates after (336 iterations in 5 s). No shipped component prescribes that pattern and `systemd Restart=on-failure` bounds it at defaults, so this is latent rather than instructed — and the direction (silent success → loud failure) is the improvement. Recorded because the wrappers also write to `hooks.log` with **no size cap**, while every emitted *hook* carries `_rotate_log` written because that file "grew without bound"; a wrapper-only spin fires no hook and so never rotates | `open` |
| P-9 | **Eight refusal causes, one exit code.** **DECIDED 2026-07-31: keep one code, distinguish in the log.** Exit 1 = refused (see the `REFUSED:` line); 0 = the run ended (see `exit_reason`). The 0–255 space is already spoken for at 126/127/128+n and `auto.sh` itself exits 130 on SIGINT, so a custom code map risks colliding with shell convention — and executed, **no mechanical consumer of a wrapper's exit status exists anywhere** in the repo or the emitted tree. The wrapper header now documents the eight causes and states that an operator-completed implementation MAY subdivide, and should document its map there if it does | `done` |
| P-10 | **O-3's stated blocker is over-scoped.** **CONFIRMED and superseded 2026-07-31.** Correct, and understated: the `exactly 13` pin reads `body(AUTO)` only, but the *protocol itself* scopes the enum to the queue runner — `Bootstrap-Protocol-v2-8-0.md:283`, *"successful per-task terminations (`max-iterations`, `goal-condition-suspect`, `terminal-success`) do not produce a queue-level `exit_reason`"*. So for `loop.sh`/`goal-loop.sh` there was never a contract question. Acting on it found a live defect P-10 did not predict: those two were logging `goal-condition-suspect` for a missing task file, an ineligible task and an already-claimed task — a goal-mode reason on `loop.sh`, which has no goal. Fixed with O-3 | `done` |
| P-11 | **Two more vacuous check-pairs, same shape as the ones repaired.** `tests/test_installer.py:363` (`F-2: auto.sh cleans .run-active sentinel`) and `:526` (`W-1: loop.sh cleans its active sentinel on exit`) both assert `not os.path.exists(...)` and pass **vacuously** on a flock-less host, where the wrapper dies at the flock guard long before the sentinel is created. A negative existence check that a refusal path satisfies for free tests nothing on that substrate | `open` — `pre-existing` |
| P-12 | **`test_auto_run_sentinel.py` (ii) no longer proves the run proceeded past the cleared sentinel.** The rewrite dropped `rc == 0` for two stderr substrings and did not replace what that assertion carried. Executed: inserting `exit 1` immediately after the stale-sentinel clear, before the `O_CREAT\|O_EXCL` claim, leaves the entire suite green. The alert-then-clear half is still pinned; the *and then it ran* half is not | `open` |
| P-13 | **The drift fix's justifying comment reasons from a task-scoped premise to a project-scoped conclusion.** It argues the old condition "could never fire in normal single-mode operation" because "a task is in exactly one mode — the wrappers enforce that". The wrappers' guard is `$TASK_ID`-scoped; the hook's globs are project-wide. One loop task and one goal task running concurrently is supported by design and *did* fire the old condition. The fix is right; the recorded reason for it is not, and a future reader auditing the glob will be misled about what it is for | `open` |
| P-14 | **The newly-blocking Stop hook has no timeout.** Four of the eight blocking gates carry one (`secrets-gate` 60, `test-gate` 600, `ci-mirror` 900, `format-lint` 120). Executed: with stdin held open, both Stop hooks are still running at 5 s. Shared-header property, identical on `cost-log.sh`, unchanged by this batch — but `iteration-summary-enforcement` is now the only Stop-family hook that can *block*, which is what makes an unbounded runtime matter. The harness's disposition toward a timed-out hook (kill-and-allow vs kill-and-block) was **not** established and should be, before deciding | `open` — `pre-existing` |
| P-15 | **The summary gate's two `ls`/`head` forks sit in `set -e`-exempt contexts.** `if ! ls …` and `latest="$(ls -t … \| head -1 \|\| true)"` are both exempt from the ERR trap, so a missing coreutil flips the gate silently — without `head` a *satisfied* gate blocks; without `ls` the gate is inert. Same T-1 class the header says was designed out of the command matcher and never applied to these. Both predate the batch | `open` — `pre-existing` |
| P-16 | **The retrofit `.gitignore` omits the lock file the retrofit wrappers actually create.** It carries a dead `.bootstrap-state.json.lock` and not `.retrofit-state.json.lock`, which the retrofit-transformed `loop.sh`/`goal-loop.sh` create via `exec 9>"$STATE.lock"`. Executed: `git add -A` stages it | `open` — `pre-existing` |
| P-17 | **The substrate-differential suite cannot detect a shell control with no SDK twin, by construction.** Every parity assertion iterates `SDK_GATES` — the smaller side — so a shell hook absent from the SDK is invisible to it. Confirmed by execution: adding an always-deny shell hook with no SDK counterpart left both parity suites at 176/0 and 96/0. This is why neither changed hook's absence from `lib/sdk_gates_template.py` registered anywhere. The SDK is a strict 7-of-15 subset and that may be correct — but the suite that reports parity should be able to say so, rather than being structurally blind to the difference | `open` |
| P-18 | **10 backlog rows still carry more cells than their table header**, so their trailing cell is dropped by GFM rendering. Measured counting only *unescaped* pipes — a `\|` inside a row is content, not a delimiter, which is why a naive count reports more. The two rows this batch introduced (D-9, I-8) and section D's stale "All `open`" caption are fixed; the remainder predate it: **A-5, A-6** (4 cells / 3), **I-11** (4/3), **J-1** (5/3), **J-10, J-17, J-19, J-20, J-15** (4/3) and **J-18** (7/3). A status silently dropped from a tracking file is how a `done` row gets re-worked | `open` — `pre-existing` |
| P-19 | **P0-3d's fix tests that the parser exited clean, not that it parsed — so a same-named different tool still fails the gates open.** `jget` accepts a parser's output whenever the pipeline's status is 0. Anything named `jq` that exits 0 without producing a parse is therefore read as a successful *empty* parse, and the gate's `case` falls through to allow. The realistic substrate is not an exotic tool but a **defensive wrapper over a broken jq** — `real-jq "$@" 2>/dev/null \|\| true; exit 0`, which is the same `\|\| true` idiom the P0-3d fix removed from `jget`, one layer out, in a file the operator owns. Executed against the emitted install with a healthy `python3` on the same PATH: `secrets-gate` on `cat .env` **rc=0 ALLOWED**, `dependency-gate` on `npm install evil` **rc=0 ALLOWED**, while jq absent / exiting 127 / `chmod 644` all give rc=2. **Bounded honestly:** two neighbouring shapes DENY and are not part of this — a swallowing wrapper over a *working* jq (the real answer is still returned) and a wrapper that prepends a banner to stdout (the polluted output still contains the command). §4.6's stated remedy is the capability probe — parse a known literal and compare — which the fix does not implement. Found reviewing PR #24, whose §7 checklist item claimed all four shapes already denied; that item now states the gap instead | `open` |

## W. Worktree isolation (issue #29, 2026-07-31)

The issue observed that this document had **no worktree entry at all**. It has
one now. W-1 itself is fixed; the rows below are the residue.

| ID | Item | Status |
|---|---|---|
| W-1 | **Worktree isolation and container-executed commands did not compose — gates tested the wrong tree, silently.** `isolation: worktree` was hardcoded on `implementer` while Phase 2 collected commands with no constraint on where they run, so under fixed-mount indirection (`docker compose exec`, `kubectl exec`, `ssh`, devcontainer CLIs) the gate compiled the main checkout while the agent worked in a worktree — green on code never built. Fixed with `commands.execute_in_cwd` + `workflow.implementer_isolation`; see `docs/changelog.md` | `done` |
| W-1a | **The load-bearing assumption is documented, not executed.** That Claude Code sets an `isolation: worktree` subagent's cwd to its worktree is taken from the tooling docs; W-1's fix was NOT gated on confirming it by running an implementer task. Direction of risk is favorable — if it were untrue the pairing would fail *more* broadly (every command, not just fixed-mount ones), so the mitigation is directionally right either way — and the emitted `implementer.md` now ships a one-command probe so each operator can settle it locally. What is still missing is one execution on this side: dispatch one `implementer` task and record where its test command ran **[Layer 2026-10-26, the v2.9.0 release closeout.]** v2.9.0 shipped with W-1a open, by the operator's ruling of 2026-10-20 (build-plan section 17), and its changelog entry and the Companion's migration note say so. | `open` |
| W-1b | **`worktree-budget.md` (G16) and the isolation decision do not consult each other.** The retrofit budget doc advises sequential over parallel worktrees above 5 GB; `_resolve_implementer_isolation` never reads `codebase_size_gb`. Two separate levers over the same behavior, each unaware of the other. Not wrong today (the budget doc is advisory prose to the agent, the config key is mechanical), but an operator can set `implementer_isolation: worktree` on a 20 GB repo and get no pushback from either | `open` |
| W-1c | **No gate asserts that a gate ran where the agent worked.** W-1 closes the configuration path into the fail-open; it does not detect the fail-open. A gate could compare its own `pwd` against `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` (or record it in `hooks.log`) so a mismatch is observable rather than inferred from a config key an operator may have answered wrong. This is the mechanical check that would have caught the defect without anyone reasoning about mounts | `open` |

## X. Issues #30–#33 residue (2026-07-31)

Four issues filed against 2.6.1 alongside #29. All four are FIXED; the rows
below are what the fixes deliberately did not do, plus defects found while
fixing them.

**Read the `X-31`/`X-32` rows with §4.9 of the security KB in hand.** Both
issues were *usability* reports (over-refusal, error in the safe direction) and
**both fixes introduced under-blocks** that only an adversarial re-run caught.
One of those under-blocks was pinned as correct in four suites.

| ID | Item | Status |
|---|---|---|
| X-30 | **The alarm hook truncated the escalation record it is documented to preserve.** `: >"$P"` on every fire, so all `.decision-pending-*` files were 0 bytes and a halted overnight loop delivered a filename and no reason. Fixed hook-side (create-if-absent + `touch`), which keeps the documented four-field contract true. Doc surfaces at §6.E, Phase 8, Phase 9.5, both Companions now state the never-truncate requirement | `done` |

| X-36h | **PARTIALLY FIXED — part 1 shipped; the substitution half is still open.** All six spellings resolve to `pip` under real bash. **Four of them say so IN THE COMMAND TEXT and now deny on both substrates**: `$'\\x70ip' install evil`, `$'\\160ip'`, `p$'\\151'p` and `pi${x:-p} install evil`. **Two still allow on both** and are the open half — `$(echo pip) install evil` and `` `echo pip` install evil `` need EXECUTION to resolve, and no anchor arm can reach them today because `cmd_segments`/`_cs_ops` split on `(`, `)` and the backtick, so `install evil` lands in a segment with no head at all. Closing them is a segmenter change touching every gate that shares the header, or a marker-collapse spelling plus a substitution-only arm — an owner call that deliberately did not ride on part 1. **NO DENY ARM WAS ADDED.** Part 1 splits the shapes by DECIDABILITY — is the word's value visible in the command text? — and treats the decidable ones as a THIRD SPELLING, the mechanism X-34 already sanctions (`cmdpos.command_spellings` now returns three, and a gate consulting an allow list refuses if ANY spelling refuses). `cmdpos.resolved_spelling` decodes the numeric ANSI-C escapes inside each `$'...'` run — re-emitting the run STILL QUOTED, so `git commit -m $'pip install evil'` stays ONE word rather than manufacturing the X-36k over-refusal — and substitutes the LITERAL branch of `${NAME:-WORD}` and its five siblings. A decoded character is accepted only if it is printable ASCII `0x21-0x7e` and not `'`, `"`, a backtick, `$` or a backslash, so a decode cannot forge a quoting construct, an expansion, an escape or a second word. **A bare `$KUBECTL`/`${PIP}` yields NO third spelling at all**, which is why the 14-of-40 over-refusal below is now structurally impossible rather than merely unobserved. Same rewrite closed three further true positives (`${SUDO:-sudo} pip install evil`, `cargo ${CMD:-add} serde`, `deno ${x:-run} <url>`) and, in a SEPARATE gate, a live secret disclosure: `cat $'\\x2e\\x65nv'` was allow/allow and is now deny/deny. **TWO DECODER DEFECTS WERE FOUND IN ADVERSARIAL REVIEW AND FIXED BEFORE MERGE, both invisible to a green suite:** (a) an octal escape is a **BYTE**, not a code point — bash masks `$'\\560ip'` to `pip` — and the two PYTHON copies read it as a code point while the shell inherited the mask from `printf`, so the SDK was MORE PERMISSIVE than the shell on 89 of the 256 values in `\\400`-`\\777`, turning a symmetric fail-open into an asymmetric one; (b) an **ESCAPED backslash is not an escape** — bash reports `\\x70ip: command not found` — but `re.sub` retries one character after a failed match, landing inside the `\\\\` pair and decoding the `x70`, so the SDK denied a command that never runs pip. Both are pinned in `cmdpos.RESOLVED_VECTORS` and swept over 12,640 escape vectors through all three implementations (0 divergent; the same sweep reports 644 against the first cut). **What is STILL not bash-exact, in the over-refusal direction only:** `\\c` and a digit-less `\\x` are consumed as two-character units by all three implementations, where bash consumes a following character (`$'\\c\\x70ip'`) — spellings are additive, so no fail-open is reachable. **The two REVERTED attempts are retained below because they are still the reason this row is not closed by a deny arm.** **The obvious fix — copy `interpreter_word()`'s "a word carrying `$` or a backtick is unresolvable" arm — does not transfer, and the cost was measured: 14 of 40 ORDINARY commands newly refused**, on both substrates, each with the unactionable message X-36b is filed for: `$KUBECTL get pods` → *"not in deps.md approved list: pods"*, `$GIT add src/` → *"src/"*, `$HELM install myrelease ./chart`, `sudo make -C $BUILD install DESTDIR=/tmp/stage`. `$VAR` at command position is ordinary and `get`/`add`/`i` are ordinary verbs; the pair is evidence of nothing. That arm works at the PIPE's command position only because a downloader upstream already narrowed the context. **Narrowing to ANSI-C quoting (`$'`) alone also failed**, for a different reason worth recording: `unquote_word` strips `'` before the anchor sees the token, so `$'\\x70ip'` reduces to `$x70ip` and the `$'` signal is destroyed — any fix keyed on that construct must inspect the RAW token, not the reduced candidate. **Part 1 did start from those two facts** — it reads the RAW token, not the reduced candidate, and it never asks whether a word is "unresolvable", only whether the text already says what it resolves to. **The remaining substitution half should too.** **A THIRD DEFECT CAME OUT OF THE SAME REVIEW ROUND AND IS A PERFORMANCE ONE, which a deny/allow corpus cannot see:** the shell transcription of `param_default` named the WHOLE command once per `${`, which is O(n²) because every bash string operation costs O(length of the string it names) — including `case "$_rest" in …`, whose word expansion copies the value; on a 126 KB value a plain `p=$S` is 1.6 ms and `case "$S" in *X*)` is 1.7 ms whether it matches at offset 0 or never. `${aN:-vN}` × 8000 (126 KB) ran 29.8 s at v2.7.1 and 86.3 s with part 1 — **a command that PASSED, turned into an unactionable timeout refusal** by the dependency gate's 60 s fail-CLOSED PreToolUse timeout, from a rewrite whose only purpose is to add DENIES. The walk now runs over a bounded window (`cmdpos.PARAM_DEFAULT_WINDOW`, rendered into the hook rather than typed there, with `cmdpos.PARAM_WINDOW_VECTORS` built to land tokens ON the boundary): 47.7 s → 0.87 s in that function. **Windowing alone does not make the property true** — what is left is a constant factor on a superlinear curve, and that still moves the crossing (`${aN:-vN}` × 11000 is 49.5 s at v2.7.1 and 60.5 s windowed-but-unbudgeted, i.e. killed) — so the third spelling also has a LENGTH BUDGET, `cmdpos.RESOLVED_SPELLING_MAX` = 16384 **UTF-8 BYTES**, rendered into both other substrates. **The unit is load-bearing and the first cut got it wrong**, which two independent verifiers found: the budget was documented as *characters* and enforced with bash `${#var}` on the shell against Python `len()` on the SDK. Those are not the same function — `${#var}` counts characters in a UTF-8 locale and BYTES under `LC_ALL=C`, because the AMBIENT LOCALE decides whether bash decodes multibyte sequences — so the same command straddled the budget differently depending on the environment the hook ran in, and a substrate SPLIT sat behind an environment variable. Measured on the emitted gates: `$'\x70ip' install evil` padded with 8000 `中` (8,023 characters, 24,023 bytes) was deny/deny under `en_US.UTF-8` and **shell-ALLOW / SDK-DENY under `LC_ALL=C`**; a 120-row boundary sweep (4 fills — ASCII, `é`, `中`, an astral emoji — × MAX−1 / MAX / MAX+1 × both units, sized to the budget in code points and in bytes, × 5 locales, two of them non-English UTF-8 built with `localedef`) found **9 splits, all under `LC_ALL=C` with a multibyte fill, 0 with an ASCII fill in any locale**. BYTES was chosen over characters for three reasons: `LC_ALL=C` is the one locale guaranteed to exist, so bytes is the only unit bash can compute everywhere (counting *characters* would require a UTF-8 locale to be INSTALLED on the machine running the hook, which a minimal container has not); bytes is the unit the budget was measured in, since every bash string operation costs O(bytes it copies) and the rung the number came from is 16,017 BYTES; and bytes ≥ characters, so the change can only SKIP the spelling more often, which restores the v2.7.1 verdict and can never add a deny. Above it the spelling is skipped, which restores the v2.7.1 verdict **exactly**: `command_spellings` is monotone, every consumer refuses if ANY spelling refuses, so an omitted spelling can remove a deny and never add one. An ODD `'` in a comment therefore leaves the walker in single-quote state across the newline; from there the single-quote arm consumes everything, the `$(` lift arm is unreachable, and `_CS_SUBST_R` comes back EMPTY. It was then verified, live, against Claude Code itself: a PreToolUse hook that exceeds its timeout is cancelled and **the tool call PROCEEDS**. So a crossing is not an over-denial at all — it is the gate being SKIPPED. The reasoning that misled this row is worth keeping visible: `dependency-gate` really is emitted with `timeout: 60` and `FAIL_CLOSED=1` (re-verified on this install: `settings.json` and `dependency-gate.sh:41`), and `docs/production-readiness.md` reads that as "a hook that runs long is a DENY the operator cannot override". But `FAIL_CLOSED=1` governs what the hook does on ITS OWN internal error; a hook killed by the HARNESS at 60 s emits nothing at all, and whether Claude Code then blocks the tool call or merely reports a failed hook is a property of the harness, not of anything this repo emits. **It does not block.** Measured with a hook that sleeps past a declared `timeout: 2` and would then `exit 2`: the hook is cancelled mid-run and the command runs, while the identical hook allowed to finish blocks. This matches the documented rule that **only exit code 2 blocks a PreToolUse call** and any other exit is a NON-BLOCKING error — a cancelled process exits 124/137/143, never 2. **So this row is a headroom row AND the mechanism behind a live bypass (X-51), and X-44's, X-47's and B3's sizing decisions were all argued against the wrong harm** — they rejected candidate sizes for causing "unoverridable denies" when the real consequence of a crossing is a skipped gate. Both halves are quadratic and unequal — the prefix costs 1281/4373/16200 us per rep at 8/16/32 KB, the REMAINDER 14131/55707/223921, i.e. **14x dearer**. ' x 34000) + 'p' + ("'z'" x 4000) + '; pip install evilpkg'` — 80022 B, 8000 jump targets, INSIDE both `_CMD_MAXLEN` 81920 and `_CMD_MAXJUMP` 8191 — is **>240 s KILLED on `bbf6434`** (the last reviewed commit) and >240 s KILLED with the `_lastw` fix; at half size (40022 B, 4000 jumps) it is the same on both. A LENGTH-cap variant, `{ { { { s` + 4000 x `'zzzzzzzzzzzzzzz'` (68009 B), is 73.39 s on `bbf6434` and 75.56 s fixed. **NOT INTRODUCED BY THE `_lastw` FIX AND NOT BY THE MEMO:** `bbf6434` predates both and is over the ceiling on every one of these. The walk's prefix is stable — only the trailing word can change — so a cached byte offset into `_CS_TAIL` plus the `_seen` state lets each call resume instead of restarting, which removes the multiplication WITHOUT caching an unstable decision. That also reaches **X-54**, whose wrapper head sets `_seen=1` and decides by EXHAUSTION — the one class an answer-memo structurally cannot serve. All four do today. | X-57 | **`_cs_isinv`'s ARRAY-phase trailing-word guard is more conservative than the invariant requires, and the exact test is O(1).** Found by adversarial plan review of the X-52 `_lastw` fix (findings 18, 26, 28); a COST item only — the shipped guard is safe, just wider than necessary. The lazy phase tests `${_tail:${#_w}:1}`, i.e. "is there anything at all after this word", which is exactly the extendable case. The array phase tests only "is this the last element", which is ALSO true when `_CS_TAIL` ends in WHITESPACE — and a tail ending in whitespace cannot have its last word extended, because the next appended run starts a NEW word. So the memo is declined for every space-separated shape that reaches the array phase, e.g. `{ { { { echo ` before a quoted run, where `echo` is provably complete. **The two phases therefore implement different predicates**, which the code comments read as if they did not. **Fix direction:** compute the tail's trailing-whitespace status ONCE at the phase switch, where an O(tail) pass is already being paid (`${_tail//[[:space:]]/ }`), and conjoin it — the per-token cost stays O(1) and no new whole-tail work is added. **Deliberately NOT taken with the `_lastw` fix**: that fix is a soundness correction and this is a cost change to the same lines, and this PR has already retracted three fixes that mixed the two. **It does NOT close X-55** — in X-55's class the runs are adjacent, so the tail never ends in whitespace and the memo is correctly declined either way | `open` — cost only, safe direction, no verdict moves |
