## Y. Layer-boundary theses — reverified 2026-08-11, both REFUTED as stated

Two architectural theses raised by the owner on 2026-08-11: (1) the steering layer is partly redundant and worth
periodically pruning while the gate layer is a durable deterministic boundary; (2) effort is an axis orthogonal to
model and belongs per-phase, keyed on the cost of being wrong. Both were put through a four-agent census plus two
adversarial critics against rendered installs before filing. **Neither survives in its stated form**, and the rows
record why, what narrower version does survive, and the single item with actual work in it.

**Read Y-3 first if you read only one** — it is the only row that proposes a change, and it pins the property the
other two both lean on. Y-1 and Y-2 are `decision` rows in the cluster-A sense: the surviving question is *"spend
anything here at all?"*, and the recommendation on current evidence is no.

**The method note is part of the finding.** The theses were plausible, internally coherent, and wrong on magnitude —
the same shape as the two wrong attributions X-45 went through before the right one. Neither was refuted by reading;
both were refuted by rendering three real projects and counting bytes.

| ID | Item | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Y-1 | **REFUTED AT MAGNITUDE. The steering layer is 0.79% of what the installer emits, is read ON DEMAND, and its generic residue is ~90 tokens — there is no cost argument here to act on.** Measured, not assumed, by calling `installer.build_plan(resolve_config(...))` on this repo's own `bootstrap.config.yaml` and summing action bodies by prefix: hooks 12 files / 1,209,183 B (84.63%), `sdk_gates` 1 file / 193,277 B (13.53%), **steering 9 files / 11,329 B (0.79%)**, skills 4,045 B, agents 2,168 B, commands 1,669 B, `CLAUDE.md` 1,191 B; total 1,428,866 B. Maximum opt-in raises steering to 11 files / 23,446 B. Line-by-line classification puts ~350 B (default) to ~2,500 B (max opt-in) in the class the thesis targets — prose that only restates what a capable model already does — i.e. **0.024% to 0.17% of emitted bytes, ~90 to ~625 tokens.** The layer called redundant is 0.79%; the layer called durable is 98.2%. **AND THE COST IS NOT LEVIED PER SESSION:** steering is read on demand, not auto-loaded — `grep -rn 'additionalDirectories' lib/` is empty, there is no `@` import, and the emitted `CLAUDE.md` says only *"Thin by design. Steering lives in `.claude/steering/`. This file is the escalation contract, not a knowledge dump."* Unread steering costs zero tokens. **THE HISTORY CLAIM IS FALSE AS STATED:** grep finds ZERO changelog events of prose pruned because a capable model already knows it. What this repo actually has a history of is **DE-DUPLICATION and contradiction removal** (`docs/changelog.md`, the entry recording that the assumption-ledger and `telemetry.md` stated CONFLICTING drift thresholds, fixed by dropping the numbers and pointing at the ledger), which is a **grep-decidable** criterion, unlike genericity, which is undecidable and model-tier-dependent. There is also one documented case of removing prose causing the regression it existed to prevent, which had to be reverted. **THE D17 ANALOGY INVERTS ON ITS LOAD-BEARING HALF.** It is fair on *emitted everywhere and already drifted*; it fails on *zero consumers, free to delete* — dead code has no consumers, which is grep-decidable and was assertion-locked (`tests/test_composition.py:125-126`), whereas prose is always consumed and its redundancy is a judgement. **THE LAYER SPLIT ITSELF LEAKS, and this is the hardest counterexample:** on the RETROFIT path steering is a RUNTIME INPUT TO SECURITY-TIER GATES. `lib/templates.py:7120-7127` states the preamble *"Reads .retrofit-state.json, steering/spec-strategy.md's legacy allowlist, and hooks/rollout-schedule.md's ROLLOUT_WEEK marker AT RUNTIME, so updating those files changes hook behavior without re-running the installer"*; the `LEGACY_ALLOWLIST_BEGIN/END` block is awk-extracted at `:7173-7175` and consumed by spec-gate-commit and tdd-gate (`:7193-7203`, `:7249`, `:7300`, `:7346`). So *"steering is model-facing prose, gates are deterministic"* is false on that path, and a pruner reformatting those markers silently widens exemptions on three security-critical gates. Likewise *"gates never compensate for a model that doesn't know better"* is false for one ADVISORY hook: the drift-detector is ledgered as *"Compensates for: Context degradation"* (`lib/templates.py:2389`) with a model-tier re-validation trigger — the BLOCKING gates do not. **THE FAIL-OPEN CAVEAT IS RIGHT BUT NEEDS ITS SHARP FORM.** *"Worse than no gate"* is literally true only for the fail-SILENTLY-open case, and this repo has that in execution-proven form — **P-19**: a same-named `jq` wrapper that exits 0 turns secrets-gate on `cat .env` and dependency-gate on `pip install evil` from rc=2 to rc=0, re-verified at branch HEAD. For a partial-coverage gate the claim is rhetorically strong and technically false (it still blocks what it covers); the operative harm is FALSE CONFIDENCE, which this repo has documented as actually occurring, and the sharper wording already exists in `docs/agentic-harness-security-kb.md` (the passage on the operator having *less* safety than they believe). Related: the *"five fixes shipped a blocker past a green suite"* figure is UNDERSTATED — KB §5.1 records **seven consecutive fix commits**, each shipping a defect into the class it was fixing, none caught by the green suite, every one caught by the next independent review. **CORRECTION TO THE BRIEFING THIS ROW WAS OPENED WITH:** there are 9 greenfield steering files by default (product, tech, deps, secrets, structure, principles, ci-cd, tools, assumption-ledger) and 11 at max opt-in (+ telemetry, design); `spec-strategy.md` and `workflow-source-of-truth.md` are **RETROFIT-ONLY**, alongside three conditional retrofit files (contracts, migration, compliance) — 16 distinct across both modes, not the 8 originally listed. **IF ANYTHING IS EVER CUT** the criterion is DUPLICATION (two file:line locations stating the same rule), never *"a capable model already knows this"*, and this exclusion list is mandatory: (i) prose named by a gate refusal message or parity-pinned; (ii) prose machine-read at runtime by a gate (the spec-strategy allowlist above); (iii) a recorded lesson from an OBSERVED model failure (X-33 / X-33c); (iv) a prose-only security control with no gate behind it (the `design.md` trust boundary, `lib/templates.py:8419-8428`); (v) the operator-facing calibration record. The largest real duplication target is the design triad (`design.md` + design-review SKILL + design-review command, 253 lines / 14,499 B = 31% of the max prose surface, stating the trust boundary three times and the invariant checklist twice) — and that duplication is **deliberate and documented**, so cutting the mirrors re-opens a prompt-injection surface. **RECOMMENDATION: no pruning pass.** The steelman that survives is narrow: *the assumption-ledger mechanism already exists for exactly the staleness class this thesis worries about, and is scoped to numeric DEFAULTS rather than prose* — extending it is the only version of this idea with a decidable trigger. | `decision` — measured and declined; reopen only with a duplication-keyed proposal that respects the exclusion list **[Layer 2026-10-28, the CLAUDE.md tidy-up closeout.]** The quote above is the greenfield line before WP4, which reworded it to "Thin by design: the escalation contract and an index, not a knowledge dump." The tidy-up (PR #133, merge `d93ceec`) removed that line and the escalation-list paragraph, so the greenfield `CLAUDE.md` has neither "Thin by design" nor "escalation contract"; the retrofit file keeps "Thin by design" (step 7 OC-6). The measurement and the decision stand. |
| Y-2 | **REFUTED AS ORTHOGONAL. Effort's baseline is set BY the model, effort is prescribed as a SUBSTITUTE for model tier, and the key the thesis proposes for effort is the key the MODEL axis already uses — so the two would be collinear, not independent.** Three refutations from the protocol's own Companion: (1) it states that xhigh effort is the DEFAULT for Opus 4.8, *"no separate flag needed"* — an axis whose zero point moves with the other axis is not orthogonal to it; (2) it prescribes falling back to *"Sonnet 5 (xhigh effort)"* for spec-review and code-review when Opus is constrained, *"compressed across fewer tiers"* — raising effort as compensation for lowering model is partial fungibility, which is coupling; (3) its stated model principle is *"match model strength to consequence-of-error x judgment-required, not to role prestige"* — **verbatim the key the thesis proposes for effort**, so the effort column would be a monotone function of the model column and carry near-zero incremental information. **THE PROPOSAL IS ALSO ALREADY SHIPPED WHERE IT MATTERS, AND A TEST FORBIDS THE REST.** The reviewer carries `model: opus` + `effort: high` on BOTH paths (`lib/templates.py:6045` greenfield, `:8079` retrofit), assertion-locked (`tests/test_installer.py:1414-1420`), and the changelog records it as KEEP-AND-LOCK, not a new capability. `tests/test_installer.py:1421-1424` **actively asserts effort appears ONLY on the reviewer**, so *"give each agent role an effort profile"* would delete a test written specifically to forbid that. The Companion's model table already annotates effort inline in four rows; exactly one of them reaches the wire as frontmatter. **THE TWO SURFACES ARE NOT PEERS.** MODEL is a real three-place operator surface — implementer / reviewer / integrator, values interpolated from `bootstrap.config.yaml` `workflow.{implementer,reviewer,integrator}_model` (sonnet / opus / inherit), assertion-locked at `tests/test_installer.py:1392-1400` plus the whole-plan SHA-256 golden. EFFORT is a single hardcoded literal at two sites with **no config key, no default, no validation and no operator surface**. **THE PHASE PREMISE IS WRONG:** the protocol's numbered Phases 0-10 are BOOTSTRAP-WIZARD phases and all three agents are created inside one of them (Phase 7); the only artifact binding roles to steps is the unnumbered per-task lifecycle, which binds implementer (gates 3-5) and reviewer (gate 6) and **never binds integrator at all**. So *"per-phase effort"* is not an extension of an existing mapping — the mapping would have to be built first. **THE MECHANICAL-STEPS EXAMPLE IS FALSIFIED BY THIS REPO.** Golden re-baselining is a numbered freeze-exception ritual whose record includes five consecutive re-baseline inventories *"written from intent and all five were wrong"*; renames have shipped parsing bugs; boilerplate emission is exactly where D17 lived. The tasks the thesis calls mechanical are among the highest-consequence here. **THE CONCRETE SAFETY HARM, and the reason this is not merely academic:** *"implementer = low effort"* would thin the artifacts four gates TRUST while changing no verdict and failing no test — the implementer authors the failing tests under TDD, and tdd-gate accepts an EMPTY file named `test_<stem>.py`, spec-gate-commit accepts any `tasks/*.md` that word-boundary-names the file, and iteration-summary-enforcement checks only non-emptiness. That is the false-confidence class in its purest form. Integrator is `model: inherit` because *"merge conflict complexity varies"*, which a fixed effort pin directly contradicts. **THE CATEGORY-ERROR CLAIM IS HALF RIGHT AND MUST BE STATED IN BOTH HALVES:** effort is irrelevant to what a gate DECIDES (verified exhaustively — see Y-3), but not to what a gate's decision is WORTH, because several gates' premises are artifacts the model writes; and the PRD twice calls the reviewer subagent part of the enforcement guarantees even though it is a model with an effort setting. Note also that frontmatter `model:` may not be honoured for nested spawns at all, which makes even the MODEL axis advisory at the platform level. **RECOMMENDATION: no change.** Close as measured-and-declined. If ever revisited, effort needs a key the model axis does NOT already use (e.g. openness of the search space, not consequence of error). Cost if taken anyway: delete or rewrite `tests/test_installer.py:1421-1424`, a three-fixture golden re-baseline, and new config keys with validation. | `decision` — coupled not orthogonal, already applied where it matters, no work proposed **[Layer 2026-10-05, WP3 (branch `feat/wp3-spec-workflow`, not yet merged), model assignment.]** Two premises moved. The reviewer now carries `effort: xhigh`, not `high` (operator decision 2026-10-05); the implementer still has no effort line, so effort still appears only on the reviewer. Refutation (1)'s "xhigh effort is the DEFAULT for Opus 4.8" does not hold for Opus 5.5, which defaults to `medium` (measured on Claude Code 2.1.289; that client's catalog gives Opus 4.8 `high`, read), and the Companion's model amendment of 2026-10-05 says so. This row's line citations predate WP3 and are not re-derived here. The recommendation is unchanged. |
| Y-3 | **THE ONE ACTIONABLE ITEM THIS PASS FOUND: the enforcement layer's independence from model and effort is TRUE, LOAD-BEARING AND UNPINNED.** Both theses lean on it (*"effort is irrelevant to the gate layer"*, *"a deterministic boundary"*), and it was VERIFIED BY EVIDENCE rather than assumed: across all 15 emitted `.claude/hooks/*.sh` and the 7 SDK gate factories in `gates.py` there are **zero LLM calls, zero model-identity reads and zero effort reads** — a strict non-comment scan for model / effort / opus / sonnet / haiku / subagent / frontmatter returns 4 hits, all inside docstrings or prose (`gates.py:320`, `:952` *"best-effort"*, `:2154`, `:2980`), and the only `claude_agent_sdk` reference is `from claude_agent_sdk import HookMatcher` (`gates.py:31`), a type import. **But nothing in the suite asserts it.** A future gate that branched on `$CLAUDE_MODEL`, on an agent identity, or on a frontmatter effort value would ship GREEN, and the property both theses rest on would be gone with no signal. **PROPOSED FENCE:** a structural test over the EMITTED surface asserting that no hook body and no `gates.py` factory references a model or effort identifier outside comments and docstrings — the sibling of the existing `_assert_tier_partition()` posture (`lib/installer.py:569-598`), which refuses to BUILD unless the tiers exactly partition the event map. **Done when:** the tripwire exists, PASSES on the current tree, FAILS on a deliberately planted `if [ "$CLAUDE_MODEL" = ... ]` in a hook body and on a planted frontmatter read, and does not trip on the 4 known benign prose occurrences. **STATE THE LIMIT PRECISELY, or the fence becomes its own false confidence:** it pins that a gate does not READ model or effort. It cannot pin that a gate's verdict is WORTH anything. Four gates are deterministic in the bytes of MODEL-AUTHORED, AGENT-WRITABLE files, and three were flipped from deny to allow during this census with a single agent-reachable write — spec-gate-commit (write any `tasks/*.md` naming the file), eval-gate (`touch .claude/.last-eval-pass`, already filed as **J-9**), tdd-gate (an EMPTY file named `test_<stem>.py`) — while iteration-summary-enforcement checks only that a model-written summary is non-empty and test-gate delegates its verdict to an arbitrary configured external program. Two further inputs sit outside *"command text plus config"* entirely and are worth their own rows if anyone wants them: **wall-clock time** (dependency-gate is superlinear and fail-CLOSED at 60 s, measured 0.27 s at 4 KB to 11.95 s at 32 KB, so the verdict is partly a function of machine speed) and **ambient locale** (`${#var}` counts bytes under C and characters under UTF-8; one shipped shell-ALLOW / SDK-DENY split came from exactly that, fixed at `_blen`, still latent at `_cs_inv_word`). | `open` — cheap, deny-direction, pins a property two theses and the layer split all depend on |

The shell gates run `commands.test`, `commands.ci_local` and `commands.eval` in a subshell of the hook, which inherits the hook's `pipefail`, its functions (`log`, `jget`, ...) and its `$$`; `gates.py` runs them with `/bin/sh -c`, which has none of them. With `commands.test: "false \| cat"` (or an everyday `pytest \| tee test.log` over a failing suite) the shell test-gate blocks the commit and the SDK test-gate allows it, a fail-open on the SDK substrate; `log hello; exit 1` writes `hooks.log` on the shell and fails with `log: command not found` in the SDK. Pre-existing (`c642731`'s SDK also ran the command through `/bin/sh`); WP2 corrected the `_user_cmd` docstring that claimed parity but did not unify the two. Fix: pick one semantics (`bash -c <quoted cmd>` on the shell, or `bash -o pipefail -c` in the SDK) and add a differential row for `false \| cat`. | `open` · T4 |

Stock macOS `/bin/bash` is 3.2, which `#!/usr/bin/env bash` reaches when no newer bash is first on `PATH`. `secrets-gate.sh` and `dependency-gate.sh` call `mapfile`, which bash 3.2 lacks, and the ERR trap turns the missing builtin into rc=2 `BLOCKED (fail-closed): unexpected hook error`, so every Bash call, and every file tool secrets-gate matches, is refused. `dependency-gate.sh` also fails `bash -n` under 3.2 at an `esac` case pattern. The `c642731` emit fails the same way, so this predates WP2, and no other WP2 hook adds a bash 4 feature. No shipped doc states a bash 4 requirement. Options for the operator: document bash 4 or later, add a `BASH_VERSINFO` preflight to the installer, or fix the gate bodies in the security work package. Do NOT state the requirement in shipped docs before the operator decides. Reproduced 2026-10-03 in the review's container (bash 3.2.57, with jq) on a WP2 default install: dependency-gate `mapfile: command not found`, rc=2, on `ls`; secrets-gate the same on a Read of `README.md`; `bash -n dependency-gate.sh` rc=2 at the `esac` pattern. With `secrets.enabled` and `deps.enabled` both `false` neither gate is installed. | `decision` — parked by the 2026-09-27 pivot (pivot-held); reopen only on an operator call · T0 on a host whose `bash` is 3.2 with either security gate on |
| Z-22 | drift-detector's state lock is best-effort under multi-second stalls during parallel tool calls (re-review C1, RR3-1, RR4-1). Parallel tool calls run the hook concurrently, and the per-session mkdir lock (`.drift-lock-<sid>`) is broken when its holder is dead, holds it 2 or more clock seconds, or leaves it pid-less for about 1 s. A holder that stalls past those bounds (load, suspend, SIGSTOP) while still running can lose an update or misattribute one: C1, a stalled holder renamed its stale state over its successor's and removed the successor's lock (fixed by the token compare, except for a stall between the token re-read and the rename); RR3-1, a holder stalled between its mkdir and its pid write took its successor's lock (fixed by `ln` without -f); RR4-1, `_unlock`'s compare and remove are two commands, so a holder that stalls between them keeps its own update, removes its successor's lock, and the successor drops its update while a third call can hold the lock beside it (disclosed in the template comment, not fixed). Separately (RR4-2), on a filesystem with no hard links (FAT32, exFAT, vboxsf, some SMB or FUSE mounts) every `ln` fails, so every call removes its own lock and runs UNLOCKED at once, logging `cannot hard-link its lock's pid file`; there, concurrent calls can lose an increment or an ack. The hook is advisory, and fix rounds 2 to 4 each produced a new race, so the driver stopped adding lock machinery. Options: accept as is; or make the release atomic with respect to ownership (move the pid file aside, compare, then remove or restore), which only narrows the window to a second stall. Rows: `tests/test_hook_behavior.py` C1 (a)-(c), RR3-1 (d) and RR4-2. | `open` · T4 |
| Z-23 | The SDK test-gate and eval-gate report a signal-killed runner's exit code as negative (final review SDK-1). When a signal kills the runner (the OOM killer's SIGKILL, or a segfault in a native extension), `_test_gate` and `_eval_gate` in `lib/sdk_gates_template.py` format the return code of `proc.wait()` unchanged, so the deny reason says `exit -9` (or `exit -11`) where the shell twin says `exit 137` (or `exit 139`). The verdict is deny on both substrates; only the code in the message differs. WP2 maps the code for format-lint-gate (`if rc < 0: rc = 128 - rc`, review TP-5) but not for these two gates. eval-gate's runner arm is new in WP2; test-gate's unmapped `exit %d` was already on `742c365`, and WP2 edited that message line. The shell's stderr also carries bash's job-control `Killed` line ahead of the runner tail, and that line reaches the model. Fix: the same mapping in both gates, and one differential row per gate with a runner that SIGKILLs itself, asserting `exit 137` on both substrates. | `open` · T4 |

| Z-29 | Routes no git hook sees. (a) A two-call `git commit --no-verify` is checked by nothing (critic Q6; accepted by the operator 2026-10-05). In block mode the refusal says "Do not bypass this check with --no-verify", and the live-run harness labels a bypass `BYPASSED`. (b) A fast-forward merge, `cherry-pick`, `revert`, `rebase` and `git am` run neither the pre-commit nor the pre-merge-commit hook (measured on git 2.55 in fix round 1; review RC-1, F6), and the `PreToolUse` fallback acts on `git commit` only. An agent's own commits were checked when it made them; what these routes move unchecked are a person's commits, which are never checked, and `--no-verify` commits. A hand-typed `git merge` of a worktree branch whose base has not moved fast-forwards, so it runs no check at all; the emitted `CLAUDE.md` says never to merge by hand. Option for both: a check at push time over the pushed range. | `open` · T4 **Corrected 2026-10-06 (WP3 fix round 2, re-review MR-2, MR-6).** (b) is wrong for a `cherry-pick` or `revert` that stops on conflicts: concluded with `--continue` or `git commit`, it fires pre-commit (measured, git 2.55); only a clean one runs no hook, and a conflicted `rebase --continue` runs none. The check then read the whole picked commit, a teammate's files included, and said to unstage them, a partial pick under the teammate's message in block mode. It now reads `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` and `REVERT_HEAD` as it reads `MERGE_HEAD`: it checks only the pick's own changes (conflicted paths, and paths where the index differs from git's automatic result, from `git merge-tree --merge-base`, git 2.40 or later; older git checks the whole picked change and says so) and never says to unstage. Below the repository top, where no git hook runs, the `PreToolUse` fallback now also acts on `git merge --continue`, `git cherry-pick --continue` and `git revert --continue` (MR-6). **Corrected 2026-10-07 (WP3 fix round 2, re-review RR-1, MR-9).** (b)'s "An agent's own commits were checked when it made them" holds only for commits made through `git commit` with the hook: `git stash` and `git commit-tree` run no hook either (measured, git 2.55), besides `--no-verify`. The merge rule (operator decision 2026-10-06 (1)) checks only the merge's own changes, so a merge brings in, unchecked, a person's commits and these. Until fix round 2 the records and the emitted merge refusal gave the rule's reason as "each side's commits were checked when they were made", which is false for the case the rule is for, a teammate's branch: measured in block mode, an agent's `git merge` of a branch that holds a person's commit of an unlisted file lands, rc 0, with no message (the re-review's two verifiers; `tests/test_wp3_records.py` derives it). The rule stands; the push-time range check above would cover what it lets in. **Layer 2026-10-12 (WP3 fix round 3, re-review 2 MR2-3, MR2-5, CRIT2-4).** (b) says `rebase` and `git am` run neither hook. That holds for their own steps and `--continue`, not for a `git commit` made while either is stopped, which fires pre-commit (measured, git 2.55). The check took the plain rule there: it listed the replayed commit's files, a teammate's included, and said to unstage them; following that left the teammate's file out of the commit, untracked, and `git rebase --continue` then completed without it (CRIT2-4). It now reads `rebase-merge/` and `rebase-apply/`: a rebase that names its commit (`REBASE_HEAD`) is judged as a stopped pick, and `git am` (`rebase-apply/applying`; it writes no `REBASE_HEAD`), or a rebase that names none, is checked whole with that reason; no remedy says to unstage. Not fixed (MR2-5): `git revert --no-commit A B` leaves `REVERT_HEAD` naming B only (measured), so the files A's revert changed read as the revert's own change (a false refusal in block mode, a false message in warn mode); the text now says "a revert that stopped on conflicts or ran with --no-commit". Option: check a revert of several commits as a whole change. |
| Z-30 | Silent loss of the git hook. `.git/hooks` is not versioned, so a fresh clone has no shim until someone re-runs the installer; neither has a project installed before `git init`, and a hook manager added later can take the hooks over (husky sets `core.hooksPath`; lefthook and the pre-commit framework rewrite `.git/hooks/pre-commit`). "$(dirname "$0")/h"`, whose `h` ends in `exit`; after the pre-commit framework's `exec`; in a comment; or another package's dispatch), and with the fallback deferring the check then ran nowhere (measured with husky 9.1.7 and pre-commit 4.6.2). On both substrates the fallback now defers only to the installer's own shim, byte for byte and executable, and only at the repository top, since the shim runs the top's check; below the top it never defers. So with husky or a hand-written hook that runs dispatch, one agent commit draws two messages again, and in block mode the fallback can refuse first. The installer no longer prints "nothing to install" or "it runs" for a hook that only mentions the line: it warns, on every re-install, that it cannot tell whether the line runs and how to confirm it, names `.husky/<hook>` for husky 9, and says when a hook is not executable. Option, unchanged: defer when git's active hook provably runs dispatch, on both substrates. **Layer 2026-10-07 (WP3 fix round 2, re-review MR-8).** The fallback also refuses falsely: it checks the whole index as it stands before the command, so in block mode it refuses a pathspec commit (`git commit -m x <paths>`) for a staged file the commit does not record (re-review MR-8, INFO; not re-measured). | Z-37 | Spec-check edges, each found by a WP3 verifier, build step or review and not fixed. (a) A `.gitignore` that ignores `.claude/specs/` keeps the task files out of the index, so every agent commit draws "No open task file exists", which names the wrong remedy (d2-check verifier, measured). (b) Cost. The matching is O(paths × entries) in a bash loop: 75.3 s at 5,000 staged paths listed in one task, against 33.5 s for the `3d866f4` hook on the same corpus, and at or below the old cost at 2,000 paths or fewer, in the covered shape (d2-check verifier, measured on the design tree, not re-measured on the build). In the refusal shape, which is also warn mode's "went through with a warning", the review measured the build at 2 to 2.6 times the `3d866f4` hook at 1,000 to 2,000 paths, and the loop about 4 times slower on bash 3.2 (review F4, INFO, not re-measured). The check also parses every task file on each commit, and done tasks never leave the index, so the cost grows with the project's history: about 5 s at 13,000 task files on the shell (review F1, measured). merge-back runs the check twice, as its gate and in the concluding commit's pre-commit hook. Fix round 1 removed the argument-length failure at that scale (batches under 64 KiB), not the cost. Git hooks have no timeout. Options: match in the one awk pass (entries in an awk array, each staged path looked up and its parent directories walked), or cache each task file's parse by its blob id. (c) `.claude/` is accepted as a prefix, and absolute `--files` paths are not refused; since fix round 1 a `--files` path that ends in `/` is refused with exit 3 (review F3). (d) In a retrofit warn week `spec-check.sh` exits 0 while it lists uncovered paths on stdout, so a `--files` or `--task` caller must read stdout; WP6's wrappers want a strict flag. (e) When a worktree's first commit lists no file of any in-progress task, the refusal lists every in-progress task; affinity over the files the branch changed since it forked would name one, with no new state. (f) The integer guard on the parsed task index has no behavioural row; its input is always an awk integer, so its mutant is equivalent. (g) The `bash -n` guard covers dispatch and the `PreToolUse` fallback (and merge-back, through dispatch), not a direct `spec-check.sh` caller such as WP6's wrappers or a reviewer's `--task` call: bash 3.2 exits 0 after a syntax error inside a compound assignment, and bash 5 exits 0 when the check's early `exit 0` comes before the error (measured in fix round 1). Option: those callers run the same guard, or the check prints an end marker they require. (h) A task file with a `working-tree-encoding` attribute (for example UTF-16LE) is read on both substrates through `git checkout-index`, which re-encodes it, so it reads as having no `Files:` line and a traced commit draws a false refusal (warn: a false message; block: refused). Pre-existing on the shell; since fix round 1 the SDK reads the same way (review SDKP-1). Options: read the raw index blob for such files, or have the installer warn when `.claude/specs` carries a `working-tree-encoding` or `filter` attribute. | `open` · T4 **Corrected 2026-10-06 (WP3 fix round 2, re-review MR-3, CRIT-1, RR-4).** (g) is superseded: the `bash -n` guard is gone. Below bash 5.2, `bash -n` does not parse inside `$(...)`, so a typo there passed it, ran on to "covered" and let an unlisted file land in block mode (measured, bash 3.2.57, 4.4.23 and 5.1.16); and it read the setlocale warning bash prints for an `LC_ALL` that is not installed as a parse error, so every agent commit was refused in block mode and went unchecked in warn mode (measured). The check now ends every verdict with the line `spec-check: end`, which dispatch and the fallback require (exit 0 or 2 without it is could-not-run), and it keeps bash's own diagnostics while it runs, so a verdict reached after one is exit 3. A direct caller that reads the end line (the live-run harness does) is covered too. RR-4: bash 5.3 also runs on past a syntax error in a compound assignment. |

Each gate again runs under `set -m`, with merge-back's own dispositions. Also since fix round 2, a stop sends the gate's process group one TERM (with CONT), waits up to 10 s for it to end, then sends KILL: fix round 1's TERM every 0.1 s re-entered a suite's TERM trap and cut its teardown short (MBR-2). Further signals are ignored until the last line, where a second Ctrl-C used to kill the run mid-stop (MBR-1), and a signal while git merges undoes the merge (MBR-3). (j) In a container whose PID 1 does not reap orphans (no `--init`), a stopped gate's processes stay in its group as zombies, so a stop can take the full 12 s (measured in docker: 12.1 s, twice). Piped to `2>&1 | tail` or `| tee`, the interruption that also ended the reader killed merge-back at its first write, before the gate was stopped: the gate ran on, the merge stayed in progress, and no last line was written. Fix round 3 measured exit 141 every time once the reader had ended, on bash 3.2.57, 4.4.23, 5.1.16 and 5.3.15; the re-review's rates depend on timing (MBS-1, CRIT2-5). (n) Fixed: interrupted while the gates ran, after a gate changed a file the merge brought in, the last line said `git merge --abort`, which git refuses; it now names the files and the way out (MBS-3). (o) Disclosed, not fixed: a Ctrl-C or a session's end during the hooks git runs before it writes the merge commit (pre-commit, commit-msg) leaves the merge in progress, and the last line says so; the re-review measured that window at about 35 to 60 ms with only the installer's hook, and seconds with a hook manager's. A signal to the script alone lets that commit finish (exit 5). The header and the note to an agent now say so (MBS-2). (h) extended: a SIGKILL also leaves the running gate going, in a process group of its own (`kill -TERM -<its pgid>`), the lock directory, which the next run takes over, and two scratch files, `bootstrap-merge-back.out.*` and `.in.*`, which a later run does not remove (it removes only its own); the header says so (MBS-4). It now prints at most 20 and then `... and N more`, and the way to drop the merge, on the signal path and where a git hook refused the merge commit, is `git checkout -- :/` (it discards every unstaged change, the listed files included), then `git merge --abort` (MB3-1). The "already in progress" remedy keeps `<the files above>`: a merge there can hold unmerged entries, and `git checkout -- :/` then fails ("path is unmerged", measured in fix round 4). (p) Disclosed, not fixed: a reader that stops early, such as an agent's `.claude/merge-back.sh <branch> 2>&1 | head -N`, kills merge-back with SIGPIPE at its next write. No last line is written, to the output or to `hooks.log`, and when that write falls between the merge and its commit, the merge stays in progress with nothing committed; the next run says so, and `--continue <branch>` concludes it (measured by the re-review: rc 0 MERGED). The header said the last line was always `merge-back: <STATUS>`; it and the note to an agent now say not to cut the output with head (CRIT3-1, MB3-4). Option, measured by the re-review: a top-level no-op `trap : PIPE` (not `trap '' PIPE`, so the gates keep the default SIGPIPE); writes then fail with EPIPE and the run finishes (rc 0 MERGED, logged in `hooks.log`). (p) is T4. **Layer 2026-10-13 (WP3 fix round 5, step 7 R4-4).** (n) extended again: the "already in progress" way to drop the merge is now `git restore --ignore-unmerged --worktree -- :/` (it discards every unstaged change, the files above included), then `git merge --abort`. `git checkout -- <the files above>` failed past 20 files, from a project below the repository top (the list gives paths from the top), and on an unmerged path ("path is unmerged"), the case fix round 4 kept it for. Measured on git 2.36.6 and 2.56 in four states (an unmerged path only, unstaged changes only, both, and run from a subdirectory); `git restore` needs git 2.23. The two `git checkout -- :/` remedies fix round 4 gave are kept: they print only once unmerged entries are ruled out, where the two commands do the same. An unmerged path is still listed twice (Z-57 (c)). **Layer 2026-10-13 (WP3 fix round 6, review of fix round 5 CRIT5-1).** (q) Fixed: merge-back's check for a git operation in progress read `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` and `REVERT_HEAD` only as files at `git rev-parse --git-path`, and a reftable repository keeps them in its ref store with no file. A stopped revert whose index and work tree matched `HEAD`, the only state the dirty check lets through, then read as none: merge-back merged, the gates passed, and its merge commit ended the revert without notice (a stopped cherry-pick was safe only because `git merge` refuses by itself while a `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` that names a commit exists; corrected by fix round 7, see its layer). With no file there, and only when `git rev-parse --show-ref-format` prints `reftable`, it now asks `git show-ref --verify` for the head; a file there still refuses, as before, so the files backend is unchanged. Measured on git 2.56: a stopped revert of one commit or of several, and a stopped cherry-pick, give NOT-STARTED on reftable, and a branch and a tag named for the heads, with no stop, still merge; `tests/test_merge_back.py` has those rows. The `reftable` guard has no row that fails without it: on the files backend with no file, `show-ref --verify` finds no head on git 2.36.6 to 2.56. Not fixed: once a revert of several commits has had its conflicted step concluded with `git commit`, no head is left but the sequencer is, and merge-back merges on either backend (Z-57 (h)). **Layer 2026-10-13 (WP3 fix round 7, review of fix round 6 CRIT6-1, RV6-1, CRIT6-2).** (q) corrected: the check now reads the two heads by the ref backend, as `spec-check.sh` does. When `git rev-parse --show-ref-format` prints `reftable`, `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` and `REVERT_HEAD` are asked of `git show-ref --verify` only and a file there is ignored; otherwise (the files backend, and git before 2.45, which prints the option back) any file there still refuses, as before, and `rebase-merge`, `rebase-apply` and `BISECT_LOG` are files on both backends. Fix round 6 refused on any file first, so on reftable a stray head file naming a commit refused the run with "finish or abort it first", which nobody could follow: git sees no operation there, `git cherry-pick --abort` and `git revert --abort` fail ("cherry-pick failed", "revert failed"), `--quit` leaves the file, and `git merge` goes ahead (measured, git 2.56). On the files backend `git merge` refuses by itself only for a `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` file that names a commit, not for a `REVERT_HEAD` one (measured, git 2.56). `tests/test_merge_back.py` has rows for a stray head file on reftable (merged) and for a `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` file naming a commit or holding garbage on the files backend (refused, as before); the backend choice now has rows that fail without it. The sequencer gap is wider than this layer's (q) said: a cherry-pick of several commits has it too, and the next `git cherry-pick --continue` lands on top of the merge (RV6-1, CRIT6-2; Z-57 (h)). The aside above, that a stopped cherry-pick was safe because `git merge` refuses by itself, holds only while a `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` that names a commit exists. |

A file the upstream project added in a directory the import moved reads as the merge's own change: ort marks it conflicted as a directory rename, and a new top-level upstream file also differs from the unshifted result. Under an enforced prefix that is a false refusal in block mode, or a false warning in warn mode. Measured on git 2.55, block mode: `git subtree pull -P lib/vendor` was refused on `lib/vendor/core/new.py`. A pull that only modifies files lands, because rename detection follows them, and `-c merge.directoryRenames=true` places a new file in an existing directory but not a new top-level one. On a full `TMPDIR` the check's two awk-written lists can therefore come out short with no error: `$TMPD/keep`, the merge's or pick's own lines, so the merge's own changes pass; and `$TMPD/parsed`, the task files, which then read as having no `Files:` block. | Z-54 | Reftable repositories (fix round 4; re-review 3 RR3-CHK-2). In a reftable repository (`git init --ref-format=reftable`, or `init.defaultRefFormat=reftable`), `REBASE_HEAD`, `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` and `REVERT_HEAD` are refs in the ref backend, not files under `git rev-parse --git-path`, and the spec check reads them only as files (`spec-check.sh`, and the SDK's `git_path` with `isfile`). A stopped rebase is then checked as a whole change: a listed resolution plus a teammate's file from the replayed commit is refused in block mode with the false reason "git names no commit it is replaying here". A stopped cherry-pick or revert falls to the plain rule, whose remedy is to unstage (pre-existing since fix round 2). Measured on git 2.55.0, both substrates. `MERGE_HEAD` stays a file on reftable. Option: read the three through `git rev-parse -q --verify REBASE_HEAD` (and the same for the other two). | `done` (was `open` · T4) — **done by WP3 fix round 5, layer 2026-10-13 (step 7 R4-1, CRIT7-1):** the row's option, on both substrates, as fix round 6 corrected it in place (review of fix round 5, RV5-SC-1): `spec-check.sh`'s `_seqref` and the SDK's `seq_ref` never look the name up. On the files backend (since fix round 7, only there; see its layer) a file at `git rev-parse --git-path <name>` is read itself, and is a stop when its first line is an object name that `git rev-parse -q --verify <line>^{commit}` finds, or a `ref: refs/` line whose target names a commit. Fix round 5 read the heads with `git rev-parse -q --verify <name>` and took a name only when `git rev-parse -q --verify --symbolic-full-name <name>` printed the name itself, so that a branch called `REBASE_HEAD` would not count; but with a branch or tag of that name beside a real stop the name is ambiguous, and the stop read as none on both backends, a regression on the files backend: the rebase text allowed unstaging the replayed commit's file, following it left the teammate's file out of the rebased commit, and the cherry-pick text said to unstage. On git 2.36.6, 2.38, 2.39.5 and 2.40, `show-ref --verify` refuses a pseudo-ref and `--show-ref-format` is not an option (rev-parse prints it back), so the second read is for reftable only; the two reads were measured right at every stop and name collision fix round 6 tried on git 2.36.6, 2.38, 2.39.5, 2.40, 2.49.1 (bash 3.2.57) and 2.56, and on reftable with 2.49 and 2.56. The row understated the effect: the refusal's remedy led an agent that followed it to drop a teammate's change. At a rebase stop fix round 4's text said git names no commit and that any other file can be unstaged, and unstaging the teammate's file left it out of the rebased commit (R4-1); at a cherry-pick or revert stop the plain rule said to unstage it (CRIT7-1). Measured on git 2.56, both backends: the ref exists at every stop where the files backend had the file (a rebase conflict, edit, `--apply`, `--rebase-merges`, a fixup, squash or reword conflict, an edit line that conflicted, a pick blocked by an untracked file, cherry-pick, revert and `revert --no-commit`), and at no exec or break stop, and `git am -3` leaves none. On the files backend every verdict and text is unchanged except one: a corrupt `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` file, which git treats as no cherry-pick, gets the plain rule, not exit 3. Since fix round 6 a file whose first line is not an object name (garbage, or a branch name) or names an object that is not in the repository is no stop, as git then lands a plain commit or refuses the commit itself, and a `ref: refs/` line is a stop, as git treats it as a cherry-pick (measured, git 2.56); a file with CRLF line endings, which git accepts, reads as no stop (Z-57 (i)). `tests/test_spec_check.py` has reftable rows for the rebase conflict, cherry-pick and revert stops, each following the printed remedy to the end, and for the edit and break stops' text; they print a `SKIP` line where git cannot init `--ref-format=reftable` (before git 2.45). Since fix round 6 it also has rows for a branch, a tag or both named for the head at a rebase, cherry-pick or revert stop and with no stop, on both backends, for the edit-conflict stop, and for the corrupt files. **Layer 2026-10-13 (WP3 fix round 7, review of fix round 6 CRIT6-1).** The ref backend decides which read, as it decides git's: when `git rev-parse --show-ref-format` prints `reftable`, `git show-ref --verify <name>` is the only read and any file at `--git-path` is ignored; otherwise (the files backend, and git before 2.45, which prints the option back) the file is read as above. Fix round 6 read a file first on every backend, and its reason, that a file which names no commit is none to git either, holds on the files backend only: on reftable git reads the heads from its ref store alone, so a file that does name a commit is none to git too. A stray `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` file naming a commit then made the check see a stopped cherry-pick git did not see: that commit's own changes were exempt, an unlisted file passed, the commit landed as a plain one, and the file stayed (measured by the critic on git 2.56; fix round 5 refused that state). Measured on git 2.56 with the fix, shell == SDK: on reftable a stray `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD`, `REVERT_HEAD` or `REBASE_HEAD` file naming a commit gives the plain rule or "git names no commit", and an agent's `git commit` there is refused; on the files backend every verdict is as at fix round 6. `tests/test_spec_check.py` has those reftable rows. The fix round 6 suites `test_spec_check` and `test_merge_back`, run unchanged against the fix round 7 code, give the same results as at fix round 6 on git 2.56, and in docker on git 2.38.0 and on bash 3.2.57 with git 2.49.1 (see the changelog's Tests paragraph). |

The remedy says that it discards every unstaged change, but no printed remedy drops the merge and keeps such a change; round 4's `git checkout -- <the files above>` discarded it too, because the list names every unstaged path. Option: restore only the paths the merge changed (`git diff -z --name-only HEAD MERGE_HEAD`), or print both remedies. (h) Once a `git cherry-pick` or `git revert` of several commits stops on a conflict and that step is concluded with `git commit`, `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` or `REVERT_HEAD` is gone but the sequencer directory stays (`git status`: "Cherry-pick currently in progress" or "Revert currently in progress"); merge-back then merges on both backends, and a later `git cherry-pick --continue` or `git revert --continue` lands the next commit on top of the merge (measured on git 2.56; not new in fix round 6; the cherry-pick case was added by fix round 7, from the review of fix round 6, RV6-1, CRIT6-2). Option: refuse while `git rev-parse --git-path sequencer` exists, which covers both. (i) A `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD`, `REVERT_HEAD` or `REBASE_HEAD` file with CRLF line endings, which git accepts (it concludes the cherry-pick), reads to the spec check as no stop (measured on git 2.56 for `spec-check.sh`); git writes LF. (j) Six files-backend rows fix round 6 added to `tests/test_spec_check.py` assert git 2.40's rule for a stopped pick and fail on git 2.38, where the check lists the whole change and says it is older than 2.40, as 11 earlier rows do; none is version-gated. The `reftable` guard on the second read, in `spec-check.sh` and in merge-back, has no row that fails without it (Z-39 (q)); since fix round 7 the guard chooses the read, and single-variable mutants of it fail rows in both directions, though not all on every git: a guard that never matches fails the stray-file rows on git 2.56, in `spec-check.sh`, the SDK and merge-back, and one that always matches fails merge-back's files-backend rows on git 2.56, but in `spec-check.sh` and the SDK a guard that always matches fails on git 2.40 and older, where `show-ref --verify` refuses a pseudo-ref, and on none of git 2.45 to 2.56 that were run (measured in fix round 7: 5 more rows fail in docker on git 2.38.0, shell and SDK alike, and none on git 2.56; git 2.41 to 2.44 not measured). Added by fix round 7 (2026-10-13), from the review of fix round 6: (k) RV6-2: the installer's test for an allowlist that "covers every prefix" (a pattern that ends in `*` and matches the prefix) is sufficient, not necessary. An allowlist of `*?`, of `*[!/]`, or of `<prefix>?*` for each prefix exempts every file under every prefix, yet fails that test, so it gets the third case's recipe, which asks for a file the allowlist does not cover, and no such file exists; fix round 5 printed "No commit can confirm the pre-commit line yet" for these (measured in fix round 7 with the installer's own probe names and a bash `case` under `LC_ALL=C`: every probe file is exempt for all three, and `src/**` for each prefix is classed covered). Hand-written patterns only. Option: also count `*?`, `*[!/]` and `<prefix>?*` as covering. (l) RV6-3: `_legacy_allowlist` reads the allowlist differently from the check's awk and trim in two cases: a file with lone carriage returns for line ends, which Python's line reading splits and awk reads as one line (the installer sees patterns, the check none), and a NUL byte in a pattern, which bash drops and Python keeps (measured in fix round 7 on the review's 11 inputs, `LC_ALL=C` as the check sets it; the review's third case, U+3000, differs only under a UTF-8 locale, which the check never runs in). The installer writes LF, so only a hand-mangled file reaches either. Option: read with `newline=""` and split on LF only. Added after the review of fix round 7 (2026-10-13): (m) RV7-1, CRIT7R-1: on the files backend merge-back refuses when a `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD` or `REVERT_HEAD` file names no commit (empty or garbage), as it did at `c1dfa5d`, while the spec check reads such a file as no stop; its remedy, "finish or abort it first", cannot be followed there, because git sees no operation. It fails closed, and only a truncated or hand-written file reaches it. The way out is to remove that file (`rm "$(git rev-parse --git-path CHERRY_PICK_HEAD)"`, or `REVERT_HEAD`). Option: refuse only when the file names a commit, with the spec check's test. Added by fix round 8 (2026-10-13), from the interactive smoke demo and the review of fix round 8: (n) the missing-start-directory arm, in test-gate and ci-mirror, covers `python -m unittest` only. `pytest tests` with no `tests/` exits 4 ("ERROR: file or directory not found: tests", measured with pytest 9.0.2), the code of a usage error, so it still blocks every commit of a new project; so does a unittest start directory whose name has a character outside `[A-Za-z0-9_./-]`, such as a space (review I3). The arm allows a red suite in three cases (review F2, C2, C3, measured on Python 3.14, shell == SDK unless noted): unittest prints the same line for any name it can import that raises ImportError while it is imported (a `tests.py` in the project, a dotted package such as `tests.unit` whose `__init__.py` fails, a `tests` package on `PYTHONPATH` or in site-packages), so those red tests are allowed when no path of that name exists; a start directory the command names wrongly (`-s test` with the tests in `tests/`, or `tests/` renamed later) is allowed with the notice on every commit, where before the fix it blocked; and the SDK, which runs the command at `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, reads the main checkout's start directory for a worktree commit, so on the first task, before the main checkout has `tests/`, it allows a worktree's red suite, which the shell blocks (Z-38 (a) records the directory difference). Also: with `-t .`, a `tests/` without an `__init__.py` blocks as "tests failing (exit 1)" even when its tests pass, because unittest then names the directory's absolute path, which exists; the notice says to put an empty `__init__.py` beside the first test (review C4, REC8-5). Option for pytest: allow exit 4 when its output says a path is not found and that path does not exist, as the unittest arm reads its own line. Option for the import cases: also require that no `<name>.py` and no path for the name with its dots read as slashes exists. **[Layer 2026-10-14, (a) done.]** `timeout-minutes` is now 30 (1,800 s) in `.github/workflows/ic-self-check.yml`; the job was not split. (a) stays as written for the 15-minute limit it names, with one exception: its "up to 534 s" is withdrawn, because none of this workflow's 344 runs up to 2026-10-14 took 534 s end to end. Refused with nothing written (exit 2 on the command line; `installer.apply_plan` raises `RuntimeError` with the same message), unless the test runners' override is set: `bin/bootstrap-install` (and the plugin copy) on a config whose `mode` is `retrofit` or that has a `retrofit:` section; `bin/bootstrap-install` on any config in a retrofit project: one that holds `.claude/.retrofit-state.json`, or one of the four files only a retrofit install writes (`.claude/hooks/rollout-schedule.md`, `.claude/inventory/README.md`, `.claude/steering/spec-strategy.md`, `.claude/debt.md`) while its first lines carry that template's signature, so a clone or worktree counts too, and so does a run from a subdirectory up to the git top level. Such a project is frozen: it keeps running the hooks it has, but it can't be repaired, re-scanned or converted, and the hooks' advice to re-run `bootstrap-install` dead-ends there. Only `--uninstall` runs. It moves the state file to `.claude/.retrofit-state.json.pre-uninstall`; where the install ran (the gitignored installer manifest is there) it also removes every marker nobody edited, so a clean uninstall releases the project, and an edited marker it keeps still freezes it, named in the refusal. A clone or worktree has no manifest, so there uninstall removes nothing and releasing the tree is the operator's call (rulings "Freeze it: only uninstall", the marker test, "Path plus retrofit signature" and "Keep a backup, then release"); `bin/bootstrap-interview` `synthesize` and `interactive` in a retrofit project or over a retrofit config; `installer.apply_plan` on a retrofit config, for a caller that skips the CLI; and `bin/retrofit-interview`, `python3 lib/retrofit_interview.py` and the plugin's module copy, on every subcommand, `--help` and a bare call. The refusal says to stop and ask the operator and doesn't name the override, which exists only so `bin/run-tests` and `.claude/mutation-gate.py` can run the retrofit fixtures (`tests/test_retrofit_disabled.py` removes it to check the refusal). Re-enabling is the operator's call once the retrofit backlog rows are revisited and built. Which rows gate it is the operator's to name; the candidate set is every row whose text mentions retrofit and whose status is neither `done` nor `no-action`, derived with ``grep '^\| [A-Z]-[0-9][0-9a-z]* \|' docs/deferred-backlog.md \| grep -i retrofit \| grep -v '`done`' \| grep -v '`no-action`'`` (35 rows at the commit that adds this row; the count excludes this row, whose own text names `done`). **[Layer 2026-10-19, the retrofit-disable closeout.]** The disable merged as `6f86266` (PR #127, 2026-10-19T00:55:25Z), so it is on `main`. Three statements above say more than holds. `--ic-checks` runs in a retrofit project too, at exit 0 and writing nothing. A retrofit install writes more files than the four markers, and one of them, `.claude/steering/workflow-source-of-truth.md`, carries a retrofit signature but freezes nothing. A run from a subdirectory is caught only in a git repository: with no `.git` ancestor, only the named directory is checked. Z-73 (h) to (j) list the other records that say the same, and Z-73 and Z-74 hold the disable's other review residuals. Both rows mention retrofit, so the grep above counts them too. Z-73 (u) records why GitHub shows only part of that command and not this layer, and why GNU grep counts more rows with the command as stored. **[Layer 2026-10-20, the markup repair.]** On the operator's ruling of 2026-10-20, the command above is one double-backtick code span whose pipes carry a backslash, so GitHub shows the whole row, and whose inner backticks carry none, so GNU grep's `-v` filters drop the rows that name `done` or `no-action`. ## Priority tiers — 2026-08-13, written against a STATED threat model

**THE THREAT MODEL, DECIDED BY THE OWNER 2026-08-13 AND LOAD-BEARING FOR EVERY
TIER BELOW: these gates exist to catch an AGENT MISTAKE, not a motivated
adversary.** Prompt injection and deliberately crafted payloads are **OUT OF
SCOPE** and are documented as such rather than silently hoped away. Anything
whose exploitation requires an attacker to author command text is deferrable
under this model; anything a well-meaning agent can trip over on its own is not.

**Sorting the backlog by discovery order is what made it look unshippable.**
These tiers re-sort it by *what a user hits first*. A row's tier is about
REACHABILITY UNDER THE STATED MODEL, not about how severe it would be if
reached.

| tier | meaning | rough |
|---|---|---|
| **T0** | blocks any use, and is not security at all | ~1 h |
| **T1** | makes shipping-with-known-risk honest rather than negligent | ~4–8 h |
| **T2** | ACCIDENT-mode risk — a well-meaning agent reaches it unaided | ~55–110 h + unsized rows (was ~30–60 h gates-only, before the 2026-08-13 readiness-`C-2`/DW/X-54/readiness-`C-3` promotions; see WHAT THIS BUYS) |
| **T3** | ADVERSARIAL-only — needs a crafted payload; **defer and DISCLOSE** | — |
| **T4** | feature gap, not a risk — ship as unimplemented, say so | — |

**T0 — `C-1`, no LICENSE [CLOSED 2026-08-14, Apache-2.0]; and `A-6`, added after review.** `A-6`
(`spec-gate-commit`'s predicate) matches T0's own definition and was in no tier
at all: it blocks the FIRST CODE COMMIT of every adopting project, which is
"blocks any use" by definition. Tier it, size it, or close it — but it cannot
be absent.

**`C-1`, no LICENSE. CLOSED 2026-08-14 — Apache-2.0**, verbatim from
apache.org, tracked, with a README statement, and pinned by
`tests/test_doc_citations.py` section 5 so deleting it is not silent. It was an
owner decision plus about an hour of work. **[Sizing corrected in the same
commit: this paragraph said "~30 minutes" while the tier table above said
"~1 h" — one document, two figures, and the tier table was right.]** It alone
held the "not production ready" verdict in `docs/production-readiness.md` in
the sense of being independently sufficient for it; **closing it does not make
the protocol ready** — the verdict now stands on the two security legs (X-37
and C-2). It stops a non-technical item from masking the real state, which is
exactly what it has now done.

**T1 — the honest-labelling pass, and the highest-leverage item on this page.**
A bypassable gate is worse than no gate in exactly one way: it changes operator
behaviour on a promise it cannot keep. The emitted `secrets.md` tells an operator
their never-read paths are blocked; under padding they are not. Writing the real
threat model into the emitted artifacts converts false assurance into calibrated
assurance, and it costs hours rather than weeks. Draft: `docs/threat-model.md`.

**T2 — accident-mode, and NOT deferrable under this model.** `C-6`
(download-then-execute: an agent can `curl … | sh` by accident, so this is not
adversarial-only) and `C-5` (the documented approval path is inert — operators
cannot configure what they believe they configured, which is a correctness defect
wearing a security costume).

**T2 (cont.) — AUTONOMY AND DYNAMIC WORKFLOWS, PROMOTED 2026-08-13 BY OWNER
DIRECTIVE.** `C-2` (the wrappers dispatch nothing) moves T4 → T2, and the
dynamic-workflow policy joins the tiering, having been absent from it entirely.

**PROMOTING AUTONOMY CHANGES THE THREAT MODEL, IT DOES NOT JUST REORDER THE
LIST — READ THIS BEFORE RELYING ON ANY T3 DEFERRAL.** The "agent mistakes"
model tacitly assumed a HUMAN BACKSTOP: someone sees each command, and the gates
are a second line. Unattended dispatch removes that assumption, and two things
follow:

* **The cost ceiling becomes ACCIDENT-REACHABLE.** X-54's shape is `sudo`
  followed by 2000 single-quoted runs — 80004 B, inside both caps. That is not
  an attack payload; it is a **bulk file operation with quoted paths**
  (`sudo chown user 'file one' 'file two' …` across ~2000 files). Supervised, a
  human would question the command anyway. Unattended, ordinary bulk work
  reaches the shape and **nobody sees that the gate was skipped**.
* **A crossed ceiling is silent.** The hook is cancelled, exits 124, and the
  call proceeds. In a loop, that failure has no observer at all.

**Therefore `X-54` is PROMOTED T3 → T2** and the owed head-class cost
measurement stops being evidence-gathering and becomes a **precondition of
shipping autonomous mode**. `X-55` stays T3: it still requires a decider
shape an agent does not construct incidentally. **`C-3` does NOT stay T3 — see
the promotion below; filing it there was this tiering's worst error.**

**Dynamic workflows — newly tiered, T2.** The DW-P4 prohibition is what stops
agents driving commands through fixture hooks, and the ledger records **two
logged breaches**. It is currently **prose, not a control**: this session had
zero violations because the prohibition was stated first in every brief, which
is a prompt-engineering result and not an enforcement mechanism. Owed, in order:
(a) `.claude/dynamic-workflow-policy.md` §1's grant table names
`~/.claude/settings.json`, which `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` makes inert — it checks a
file that cannot affect this tree; (b) the breach count is stale against the
ledger; (c) **decide explicitly whether DW-P4 stays advisory or gets a
mechanical control, and write the answer down.** Permitting dynamic workflows as
a first-class use while their only safeguard is prose is the same
false-assurance shape T1 exists to eliminate.

**T2 (cont.) — `C-3` (readiness), PROMOTED T3 → T2 2026-08-13 after adversarial
review, and the correction is worth stating in full because the original tiering
was wrong in the dangerous direction.** **DISAMBIGUATION FIRST: this is
`docs/production-readiness.md`'s C-3, "Nothing protects the gate substrate from
itself" — NOT this file's own cluster-C row C-3 ("Doc-citation normalization
pass", :52).** The two namespaces collide and every earlier reference here was
unglossed.

It was filed T3 as "adversarial-only". **That is false of it.** Measured
end-to-end with a positive control: the five-hook Bash chain denies `cat .env`,
then `printf "exit 0" > .claude/hooks/secrets-gate.sh` is **allowed by all
five**, secrets-gate becomes 6 bytes, and `cat .env` returns the canary — **and
the same overwrite works through the Write tool.** One ordinary write, no
payload, no density tuning, no decider shape. It is a TOTAL gate defeat and it
is the cheapest item on this page to reach.

Accident-reachability is not hypothetical here: this repo's own ledger records
**two** cases of well-meaning agents violating an explicit prohibition —
`.claude/trust-ramp.md` logs a DW-P4 breach (agents drove commands through
fixture hooks, 53 invocations) and a later round where "several wrote diff files
into the session scratchpad despite an explicit read-only prohibition stated
twice". Neither agent was an adversary. **Honest limit: nobody has yet MEASURED
an agent accidentally overwriting a live gate hook.** The case rests on one
write sufficing (measured) plus two logged prohibition violations, not on an
observed incident.

**T3 — deferred WITH THE REASON RECORDED, not forgotten.** `X-55` and the
remaining padding/cost classes. Each needs a deliberately constructed payload or
a specific decider shape. **They are `open` and stay `open`**;
deferring is a scope decision, not a claim they are fixed. If the threat model
widens to include prompt injection, THESE BECOME BLOCKING IMMEDIATELY, because
injected content makes command text attacker-controlled.

**T4 — feature gaps.** `C-7` only: the SDK substrate has never run against the
real SDK. **Shell is the supported substrate.**

**WHAT THIS BUYS:** with autonomy and dynamic workflows promoted, roughly
**70–120 h** rather than the 35–70 h of the gates-only release — still well
under the 150–300 h of fixing everything, because `X-55`, readiness-`C-3` and
`C-7` remain *must disclose* rather than *must fix* — readiness-`C-3` sits in
T2 for disclosure-and-operational-mitigation priority (make `.claude/hooks/**`
and `.claude/settings.json` agent-read-only by means outside the gates,
threat-model §3); its in-tree self-protection fix stays unpriced here. The
delta is readiness-`C-2` (~20–40 h), the dynamic-workflow policy (~5–10 h),
and `X-54` plus the head-class measurement that sizes it.

**ONE PREMISE THAT DOES NOT SURVIVE, recorded so it is not re-argued:** "better
models mean smaller risk" is half true and half inverted. Better models reduce
ACCIDENTAL bad commands — which is the surface these gates already cover well.
They do nothing for prompt injection (a more instruction-following model can be
MORE steerable by injected content), and nothing at all for the padding bypasses,
which are deterministic properties of a bash tokenizer's cost curve with the
model nowhere in them. The case for shipping rests on the threat model, not on
model quality.

## Priority reading

Highest-signal actionable clusters: **B** (seam follow-ups) and **E** (smoke /
cross-mode coverage). **P-1** is the highest-impact open item overall — a
safety fix that does not reach the installs that need it. **W-1a** is cheap and
should be done before the next release that touches worktrees; **W-1c** is the
mechanical detector W-1 deliberately did not build. **X-32i** was the one
genuinely-open new fail-open this batch surfaced; it is **closed** (issue #36,
fixed by making `python -m` a command-position prefix). **X-31** and
**X-32** are `decision` rows, not open work: their exemptions were attempted
over four rounds and removed, and reopening them is an owner call about cost —
read KB §4.9 before spending anything there. **A**, **F-4**, **O-3** and
**P-9** are decisions only. **D** and **G** are mostly small cleanups. **H** is
effectively closed. B-2 and B-3 are blocked on the Tessera roadmap, not on us.
**Y-3** is the cheapest deny-direction item on this page: it pins, with a
tripwire, the model/effort independence of the enforcement layer that **Y-1**
and **Y-2** both turned out to depend on. **Y-1** and **Y-2** themselves are
closed-unless-reopened decisions — both theses were measured and declined.
